西非 猴痘 的五个十年:历史、流行病学、病毒 进化 和 储存宿主 生态学(1970-2025)

Five Decades of Mpox in West Africa: History, Epidemiology, Viral Evolution, and Reservoir Ecology (1970–2025)
作者： Adeyinka Jeremy Adedeji, Ishaku Leo Elisha, Ismaila Shittu, Dennis Kabantiyok, Olanrewaju Eyitayo Igah, Nicodemus Mkpuma, Nanven Abraham Maurice, Yushau Umar, Jolly Amoche Adole, Moses Oguche
摘要中文翻译从历史上看,猴痘 被认为是一种受地理限制的“贫困病”,导致全球卫生行为者数十年的忽视。由于天花 疫苗接种 停止而导致的人群免疫力下降以及长期的科学忽视,为猴痘病毒(MPXV)的神秘适应创造了条件。这最终导致了前所未有的全球公共卫生危机的出现。本综述旨在系统地追溯 1970 年至 2025 年西非 流行病学 的历史、流行病学、基因组 进化 和 猴痘 生态。按照 PRISMA 指南,110 篇符合纳入标准的文章被合成,以绘制病毒的轨迹。近四十年来,“沉默时代”(1970-2016)掩盖了西非野生动物(主要是啮齿动物和小型哺乳动物)中 MPXV 的无声传播。这种流行病学的平静随着 2017 年尼日利亚的重新出现而结束,这标志着根本性的范式转变。疾病特征从散发的农村儿童感染转变为年轻成年男性中持续的城市继发性 传播。这种转变通常与性网络有关,尤其是在男男性行为者中,其特点是新的临床表现,包括生殖器和肛周病变。基因组分析显示,大约 3500 年前,进化枝 II 与进化枝 I 分叉,并且通过删除补体结合蛋白等毒力因子来独特定义。重要的是,引发 2022 年全球 暴发 的进化枝 IIb 谱系表现出与 APOBEC3 介导的超突变一致的加速微进化。这种 宿主 驱动的突变特征提供了基因组证据,支持这样的假设:分支 IIb 早在 2014 年就在西非人与人之间的 传播 链中神秘地循环。从生态角度来看,虽然尚未确定明确的 储存宿主,但有证据表明啮齿类动物多种多样,并且 宿主 范围不断扩大。猴痘 从罕见的 人兽共患病 转变为全球威胁,凸显了延迟干预的严重后果,需要强大、综合的“同一个健康”监测。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述考察了 1970 年至 2025 年西非境内的猴痘病毒 (MPXV)、其分支(分支 I、分支 II、分支 IIb)和 猴痘 疾病,重点关注历史忽视、流行病学变化和基因组 进化 如何导致该病毒从罕见的 人兽共患病 转变为全球公共卫生威胁的核心问题。
证据基础与方法
这是根据 PRISMA 指南进行的系统评价,综合了 110 篇纳入的文章,以绘制西非 MPXV 的轨迹。该综述追溯了 1970 年至 2025 年 猴痘 的历史、流行病学、基因组和生态维度。
共识与结论
主要发现包括确定了一个“沉默时代”(1970-2016),其特点是西非野生动物(主要是啮齿动物和小型哺乳动物)中 MPXV 的神秘地方性传播。2017 年尼日利亚的重新出现标志着一种范式转变,猴痘 从零星的农村儿科感染转变为持续的城市 传播,特别是在年轻成年男性中。基因组分析表明,进化枝 II 在大约 3500 年前与进化枝 I 分化,其中进化枝 IIb 表现出与 APOBEC3 介导的超突变一致的加速微进化,这表明早在 2014 年,人与人之间的 传播 链中就存在神秘循环。
争议与缺口
该综述强调了尚未解决的生态问题,包括尽管有证据表明啮齿类动物多种多样且 宿主 范围不断扩大,但缺乏对 MPXV 的明确 储存宿主 鉴定。此外,从人畜共患 跨物种溢出 到持续人类 传播 转变的机制仍然知之甚少,特别是关于性网络在驱动 传播 动态中的作用。
科研与实践启示
该审查强调迫切需要集成“One Health”监测 系统来检测和应对神秘的 MPXV 循环。它呼吁增强基因组监测来监测病毒进化,特别是APOBEC3驱动的突变,并呼吁对储存宿主生态和传播机制进行有针对性的研究,以为公共卫生干预和风险评估提供信息。

Five Decades of Mpox in West Africa: History, Epidemiology, Viral Evolution, and Reservoir Ecology (1970–2025)

Authors: Adeyinka Jeremy Adedeji, Ishaku Leo Elisha, Ismaila Shittu, Dennis Kabantiyok, Olanrewaju Eyitayo Igah, Nicodemus Mkpuma, Nanven Abraham Maurice, Yushau Umar, Jolly Amoche Adole, Moses Oguche
Original abstract (English)
Historically, mpox was thought to be a geographically constrained ‘disease of poverty,’ leading to decades of neglect by global health actors. Waning population immunity from the cessation of smallpox vaccination and prolonged scientific neglect created conditions that enabled the monkeypox virus (MPXV) to adapt cryptically. This ultimately contributed to the emergence of unprecedented global public health crises. This review aims to systematically trace the history, epidemiology, genomic evolution, and reservoir ecology of mpox in West Africa from 1970 to 2025. Following PRISMA guidelines, 110 articles met the inclusion criteria and were synthesized to map the virus’s trajectory. For nearly four decades, an “Era of Silence” (1970–2016) masked the silent enzootic circulation of MPXV within West African wildlife, primarily rodents and small mammals. This epidemiological quiescence ended with the 2017 re-emergence in Nigeria, which signaled a fundamental paradigm shift. The disease profile transitioned from sporadic, rural paediatric infections to sustained, urban and secondary transmission among young adult males. This shift was often associated with sexual networks, especially among men who have sex with men, and was characterized by novel clinical presentations, including genital and perianal lesions. Genomic analyses revealed that clade II diverged from clade I approximately 3500 years ago and is uniquely defined by the deletion of virulence factors, such as the complement-binding protein. Importantly, the clade IIb lineage, which triggered the 2022 global outbreak, exhibits accelerated microevolution consistent with APOBEC3-mediated hypermutation. This host-driven mutational signature provides genomic evidence supporting the hypothesis that clade IIb circulated cryptically within human-to-human transmission chains in West Africa as early as 2014. Ecologically, while no definitive reservoir has yet been identified, evidence suggests diverse rodents and an expanding host range. The transformation of mpox from a rare zoonosis to a global threat underscores the severe consequences of delayed intervention, demanding robust, integrated “One Health” surveillance.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines the monkeypox virus (MPXV), its clades (clade I, clade II, clade IIb), and mpox disease within West Africa from 1970 to 2025, focusing on the central question of how historical neglect, epidemiological shifts, and genomic evolution contributed to the virus's transition from a rare zoonosis to a global public health threat.
Evidence base and method
This is a systematic review conducted following PRISMA guidelines, synthesizing 110 included articles to map the trajectory of MPXV in West Africa. The review traces historical, epidemiological, genomic, and ecological dimensions of mpox from 1970 to 2025.
Consensus and conclusions
Key findings include the identification of an 'Era of Silence' (1970–2016) marked by cryptic enzootic circulation of MPXV in West African wildlife, primarily rodents and small mammals. The 2017 re-emergence in Nigeria signaled a paradigm shift, with mpox transitioning from sporadic rural pediatric infections to sustained urban transmission, particularly among young adult males. Genomic analyses indicate clade II diverged from clade I approximately 3500 years ago, with clade IIb exhibiting accelerated microevolution consistent with APOBEC3-mediated hypermutation, suggesting cryptic circulation within human-to-human transmission chains as early as 2014.
Controversies and gaps
The review highlights unresolved ecological questions, including the lack of definitive reservoir identification for MPXV despite evidence suggesting diverse rodents and an expanding host range. Additionally, the mechanisms underlying the shift from zoonotic spillover to sustained human transmission remain poorly understood, particularly regarding the role of sexual networks in driving transmission dynamics.
Research and practice implications
The review underscores the urgent need for integrated 'One Health' surveillance systems to detect and respond to cryptic MPXV circulation. It calls for enhanced genomic surveillance to monitor viral evolution, particularly APOBEC3-driven mutations, and for targeted research into reservoir ecology and transmission mechanisms to inform public health interventions and risk assessment.

A 高优先级学术文献 · 综述A high priorityAcademic literature · Review

2025 年乌干达正在进行的 暴发 大型宗教群众集会期间 猴痘 的综合症 监测:一项横断面研究

Syndromic surveillance for mpox during a large religious mass gathering amid an ongoing outbreak, Uganda, 2025: a cross-sectional study
作者： Justine Wobusobozi, Richard Migisha, Martha Dorcas Nalweyiso, Winfred Nakaweesi, Aminah Namwabira, Kyomugisha Denise Aman, Bernard Lubwama, Lilian Bulage, Benon Kwesiga, Alex Riolexus Ario
摘要中文翻译大型宗教群众集会通过拥挤、长时间密切接触、共用设施和广泛的人口流动,可能助长传染病。2025 年乌干达烈士日纪念活动是在正在进行的全国 猴痘 暴发 活动期间举行的。我们描述了在大型宗教群众集会期间对 猴痘 进行综合征 监测 的实施情况,以及在朝圣者中检测到的 猴痘 兼容疾病的产生情况。我们进行了横断面症状筛查调查,并回顾性审查了纳穆贡戈天主教和新教圣地2025年5月29日至6月5日期间的紧急医疗服务(EMS)记录。通过在入口处系统抽样和在划定区域内随机抽样,选择了年龄≥18岁的朝圣者。猴痘 兼容疾病被定义为急性发热([≥]38.5{°C),伴有皮疹以及头痛、淋巴结肿大、背痛、肌痛或极度虚弱中的至少一种。我们总结了寻求护理的朝圣者的参与者特征、报告的症状、猴痘 兼容疾病和临时诊断。在 1,523 名朝圣者中,有 1,299 人参与(回应率为 85.3%);中位年龄为 35 岁(四分位距:25-49),其中 752 名(57.9%)为男性。在积极筛查的朝圣者中,73 名 (5.6%) 报告了 [≥]2 猴痘 相关症状,7 名 (0.5%) 符合 猴痘 兼容疾病定义。在 5,216 名寻求治疗的朝圣者中,有 3,415 人(65.5%)记录出现症状;其中,628 人(18.4%)有 猴痘 相关症状。在所有 5,216 名寻求护理者中,最常见的临时诊断是消化性溃疡病(n=563;10.8%)、呼吸道感染(n=482;9.2%)、疟疾(n=190;3.6%)、尿路感染(n=96;1.8%)和胃炎(n=79;1.5%)。主动症状筛查表明,一般朝圣者人群中 患病率 的 猴痘 兼容疾病较低,而 EMS 记录在寻求护理的朝圣者中发现了更大的症状信号。将这两种方法与标准化文件、临床分诊、实验室确认和随访相结合可以改善群众集会期间的检测
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述审查了 2025 年乌干达一次大型宗教群众集会期间对 猴痘病毒 的症状 监测 的使用情况,重点关注这样的核心问题:在正在进行的全国 猴痘 暴发 期间,这种 监测 是否可以检测到朝圣者中与 猴痘 兼容的疾病。
证据基础与方法
该审查描述了 2025 年 5 月 29 日至 6 月 5 日在纳穆贡戈天主教和新教圣地进行的横断面症状筛查调查和对紧急医疗服务 (EMS) 记录的回顾性审查,采用系统和随机抽样方法,对 18 岁或以上的朝圣者进行调查。
共识与结论
主动症状筛查在主动筛查的朝圣者中发现了低 患病率 的 猴痘 兼容疾病 (0.5%),而寻求护理者中的 EMS 记录显示出更大的症状信号(18.4% 具有 猴痘 相关的表现),这表明寻求护理人群中的症状 监测 可能在群众集会期间产生更高的检出率。
争议与缺口
该审查强调了主动筛查和 EMS 记录在检测 猴痘 兼容疾病方面的差异,表明 猴痘 敏感性或病例确定方法的潜在局限性。此外,该研究并未报告所有综合征病例的实验室确认,这使得 猴痘 的真实负担存在不确定性。
科研与实践启示
该综述表明,将主动症状筛查与标准化记录、临床分诊、实验室确认和随访相结合可以改善群众集会期间的 猴痘 检测,特别是通过在寻求护理的人群中利用综合征型 猴痘。

Syndromic surveillance for mpox during a large religious mass gathering amid an ongoing outbreak, Uganda, 2025: a cross-sectional study

Authors: Justine Wobusobozi, Richard Migisha, Martha Dorcas Nalweyiso, Winfred Nakaweesi, Aminah Namwabira, Kyomugisha Denise Aman, Bernard Lubwama, Lilian Bulage, Benon Kwesiga, Alex Riolexus Ario
Original abstract (English)
Large religious mass gatherings can facilitate infectious disease transmission through crowding, prolonged close contact, shared facilities, and extensive population movement. The 2025 Uganda Martyrs Day commemoration occurred during an ongoing national mpox outbreak. We describe the implementation of syndromic surveillance for mpox during a large religious mass gathering and the yield of mpox-compatible illness detected among pilgrims. We conducted a cross-sectional symptom-screening survey and retrospectively reviewed emergency medical services (EMS) records from the Namugongo Catholic and Protestant shrines during 29 May-5 June 2025. Pilgrims aged [≥]18 years were selected through systematic sampling at entrance gates and random sampling within demarcated zones. Mpox-compatible illness was defined as acute fever ([≥]38.5{degrees}C) with rash and at least one of headache, lymphadenopathy, back pain, myalgia, or profound weakness. We summarized participant characteristics, reported symptoms, mpox-compatible illness, and provisional diagnoses among pilgrims seeking care. Of 1,523 pilgrims approached, 1,299 participated (85.3% response rate); median age was 35 years (interquartile range: 25-49), and 752 (57.9%) were male. Among actively screened pilgrims, 73 (5.6%) reported [≥]2 mpox-related symptoms, and seven (0.5%) met the mpox-compatible illness definition. Among 5,216 pilgrims who sought care, 3,415 (65.5%) had documented presenting symptoms; of these, 628 (18.4%) had mpox-related syndromic presentations. Among all 5,216 care-seekers, the most common provisional diagnoses were peptic ulcer disease (n=563; 10.8%), respiratory tract infection (n=482; 9.2%), malaria (n=190; 3.6%), urinary tract infection (n=96; 1.8%), and gastritis (n=79; 1.5%). Active symptom screening suggested a low prevalence of mpox-compatible illness in the general pilgrim population, whereas EMS records identified a larger syndromic signal among pilgrims who sought care. Combining both approaches with standardized documentation, clinical triage, laboratory confirmation, and follow-up could improve detection during mass gatherings
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines the use of syndromic surveillance for mpox virus during a large religious mass gathering in Uganda in 2025, focusing on the central question of whether such surveillance can detect mpox-compatible illness among pilgrims during an ongoing national mpox outbreak.
Evidence base and method
The review describes a cross-sectional symptom-screening survey and retrospective review of emergency medical services (EMS) records conducted at the Namugongo Catholic and Protestant shrines from 29 May to 5 June 2025, using systematic and random sampling methods among pilgrims aged 18 years or older.
Consensus and conclusions
Active symptom screening identified a low prevalence of mpox-compatible illness (0.5%) among actively screened pilgrims, while EMS records among care-seekers showed a larger syndromic signal (18.4% with mpox-related presentations), suggesting that syndromic surveillance in care-seeking populations may yield higher detection rates during mass gatherings.
Controversies and gaps
The review highlights a discrepancy between active screening and EMS records in detecting mpox-compatible illness, indicating potential limitations in surveillance sensitivity or case ascertainment methods. Additionally, the study does not report laboratory confirmation for all syndromic cases, leaving uncertainty about the true burden of mpox.
Research and practice implications
The review suggests that combining active symptom screening with standardized documentation, clinical triage, laboratory confirmation, and follow-up could improve mpox detection during mass gatherings, particularly by leveraging syndromic surveillance in care-seeking populations.

非洲的 猴痘病毒 分支 进化 和 流行病学:遗传变异和临床影响的系统评价

Mpox virus clade evolution and epidemiology in Africa: a systematic review of genetic variability and clinical impact
作者： Timothy Nathaniel Yakubu
摘要中文翻译摘要 背景:猴痘病毒 (MPXV),以前称为猴痘病毒,在流行和非流行地区重新出现,部分原因是不同的遗传谱系:进化枝 1 和进化枝 2。目的:本系统综述综合了非洲 MPXV 基因组变异及其与临床严重程度、传播 动态和 暴发 模式的关系的最新证据。方法:按照 PRISMA 指南,我们检索了 Semantic Sc​​holar、PubMed、Google Scholar 和其他相关数据库,以识别比较 Clade 1 和 Clade 2 的研究。数据提取包括 突变 分析、暴发 大小和 传播 动态。结果:十一项研究符合纳入标准。分支 1,主要分布在中非和东非,表现出毒力基因(D14L,补体控制蛋白)的关键缺失和高 死亡率 率(高达 10%)。分支 2 在西非和全球流行,表现出广泛的 APOBEC3 型 突变、移码和串联重复,疾病较轻,人与人之间的 传播 增强。结论:毒力和免疫逃避基因的遗传差异从根本上影响 猴痘 进化枝中的 暴发 特征。改进 基因组监测 对于公共卫生准备工作至关重要。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该审查重点关注 猴痘病毒 (MPXV)(以前称为猴痘病毒),检查其在非洲的分支 进化 和 流行病学。核心问题是 MPXV 进化枝的基因组变异与其临床严重程度、传播 动态和 暴发 模式之间的关系。
证据基础与方法
该综述遵循 PRISMA 指南并检索了 Semantic Sc​​holar、PubMed、Google Scholar 和其他相关数据库。资格标准包括比较 MPXV 分支 1 和分支 2 的研究,数据提取侧重于 突变 分析、暴发 大小和 传播 动态。
共识与结论
分支 1,主要分布在中非和东非,表现出毒力基因(D14L,补体控制蛋白)的关键缺失和高 死亡率 率(高达 10%)。分支 2 在西非和全球流行,表现出广泛的 APOBEC3 型 突变、移码和串联重复,疾病较轻,人与人之间的 传播 增强。毒力和免疫逃避基因的遗传差异从根本上影响 猴痘 进化枝的 暴发 特征。
争议与缺口
该综述指出了证据的异质性,关于特定 传播 动态和某些分支的临床结果的数据有限。缺乏关于特定分支的地理分布及其对 暴发 模式的影响的详细信息。
科研与实践启示
改进 基因组监测 对于公共卫生准备工作至关重要。该综述强调需要进一步研究 MPXV 分支的遗传变异及其对疾病严重程度和 传播 的影响。

Mpox virus clade evolution and epidemiology in Africa: a systematic review of genetic variability and clinical impact

Authors: Timothy Nathaniel Yakubu
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Background: The Mpox virus (MPXV), formerly known as monkeypox virus, is re-emerging in endemic and non-endemic regions, driven in part by divergent genetic lineages: Clade 1 and Clade 2. Objective : This systematic review synthesizes current evidence on the genomic variability of MPXV in Africa and its relationship to clinical severity, transmission dynamics, and outbreak patterns. Methods : Following PRISMA guidelines, we searched Semantic Scholar, PubMed, Google Scholar, and other relevant databases to identify studies comparing Clade 1 and Clade 2. Data extraction included mutation profiling, outbreak size, and transmission dynamics. Results : Eleven studies met inclusion criteria. Clade 1, predominant in Central and East Africa, exhibits key deletions in virulence genes (D14L, complement control protein) and high mortality rates (up to 10%). Clade 2, prevalent in West Africa and globally, shows extensive APOBEC3-type mutations, frameshifts, and tandem repeats, with milder disease and enhanced human-to-human transmission. Conclusion : Genetic differences in virulence and immune evasion genes fundamentally influence outbreak characteristics across Mpox clades. Improved genomic surveillance is critical for public health preparedness.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on the Mpox virus (MPXV), formerly known as monkeypox virus, examining its clade evolution and epidemiology in Africa. The central question is the relationship between genomic variability of MPXV clades and their clinical severity, transmission dynamics, and outbreak patterns.
Evidence base and method
The review followed PRISMA guidelines and searched Semantic Scholar, PubMed, Google Scholar, and other relevant databases. The eligibility criteria included studies comparing Clade 1 and Clade 2 of MPXV, with data extraction focusing on mutation profiling, outbreak size, and transmission dynamics.
Consensus and conclusions
Clade 1, predominant in Central and East Africa, exhibits key deletions in virulence genes (D14L, complement control protein) and high mortality rates (up to 10%). Clade 2, prevalent in West Africa and globally, shows extensive APOBEC3-type mutations, frameshifts, and tandem repeats, with milder disease and enhanced human-to-human transmission. Genetic differences in virulence and immune evasion genes fundamentally influence outbreak characteristics across Mpox clades.
Controversies and gaps
The review notes heterogeneity in the evidence, with limited data on specific transmission dynamics and clinical outcomes for certain subclades. There is a lack of detailed information on the geographic distribution of specific subclades and their impact on outbreak patterns.
Research and practice implications
Improved genomic surveillance is critical for public health preparedness. The review highlights the need for further research on the genetic variability of MPXV clades and their implications for disease severity and transmission.

深入了解猴痘病毒感染的生命周期和治疗药物

Insights into the Life Cycle and Therapeutic Agents for Monkeypox Virus Infection
作者： Qiuyu Zhong, Shulei Hu, Xiong Xie, Xiaofei He, Jinlin Wang, Haowen Shou, Wenhao Dai, Hong Liu
摘要中文翻译摘要 自 1970 年出现第一例确诊病例以来,猴痘病毒(MPXV)已成为对全球公共卫生的重大威胁。世界卫生组织(WHO)曾两次宣布其为国际关注的突发公共卫生事件(PHEIC)。尽管进行了数十年的研究,但只有 特考韦瑞 获得欧洲药品管理局 (EMA) 批准用于治疗 MPXV 感染的 治疗。MPXV 和其他 正痘病毒 (OPXV) 之间的 基因组 和结构相似,表明用于其他 OPXV 的策略可能适用于 MPXV。这篇综述系统地总结了OPXV,特别是MPXV的基因组、结构和生命周期的关键阶段。根据其不同的作用机制讨论了多种针对 MPXV 和其他 OPXV 的 抗病毒药物 药物:1) 阻断病毒进入和融合,2) 抑制 DNA 复制和加工,3) 破坏转录和 mRNA 加工,4) 防止病毒体组装、成熟和释放,5) 调节 免疫应答,6) 机制未知。总体而言,本文对 MPXV 潜在治疗靶点和药物的当前研究进展进行了系统综述,旨在为开发针对 猴痘 的有效治疗药物提供创新见解和策略。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述重点关注猴痘病毒 (MPXV) 的生命周期及其 治疗 可用的治疗药物。核心问题是系统总结当前MPXV潜在治疗靶点和药物的研究进展,旨在为开发针对猴痘的有效治疗药物提供创新见解和策略。主题边界包括 MPXV 分支和 猴痘 疾病,相关病毒如天花病毒、天花、牛痘病毒、痘苗病毒、传染性软疣病毒和骆驼痘病毒被视为补充,除非也存在具体目标证据。
证据基础与方法
该综述系统地总结了基因组、结构以及正痘病毒(OPXV)特别是MPXV生命周期中的关键阶段。它根据不同的作用机制讨论了针对 MPXV 和其他 OPXV 的多种 抗病毒药物 药物,包括阻断病毒进入和融合、抑制 DNA 复制和加工、破坏转录和 mRNA 加工、防止病毒粒子组装、成熟和释放、调节 免疫应答 和未知机制。该综述没有明确报告荟萃分析或共识过程。
共识与结论
该评论强调,MPXV 的 基因组 和结构与其他 正痘病毒 (OPXV) 相似,这表明用于其他 OPXV 的策略可能适用于 MPXV。它还指出,只有特考韦瑞已获得欧洲药品管理局(EMA)批准用于MPXV感染的治疗。
争议与缺口
审查指出,虽然 MPXV 的 基因组 和结构与其他 OPXV 相似,但其他 OPXV 使用的策略对 MPXV 的适用性仍然是一个不确定的领域。此外,该综述没有提供所讨论的各种 抗病毒药物 药物作用机制的具体细节,在了解其功效和对抗 MPXV 的潜力方面留下了一些证据空白。
科研与实践启示
该综述表明,需要进一步研究来探索 抗病毒药物 药物对抗 MPXV 的潜力,特别是那些作用机制尚未完全了解的药物。鉴于目前可用的选择有限,它还强调了开发新的 MPXV 治疗药物的重要性。本文旨在为该领域未来的研究和开发工作提供基础。

Insights into the Life Cycle and Therapeutic Agents for Monkeypox Virus Infection

Authors: Qiuyu Zhong, Shulei Hu, Xiong Xie, Xiaofei He, Jinlin Wang, Haowen Shou, Wenhao Dai, Hong Liu
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Since the first confirmed case in 1970, the monkeypox virus (MPXV) has emerged as a significant threat to global public health. The World Health Organization (WHO) has declared it a Public Health Emergency of International Concern (PHEIC) on two occasions. Despite decades of research, only tecovirimat has been approved by the European Medicines Agency (EMA) for the treatment of MPXV infection. The genome and structure are similar between MPXV and other orthopoxviruses (OPXVs), suggesting that the strategies used for other OPXVs may be applicable to MPXV. This review systematically summarizes the genome, structure, and critical stages in the life cycle of OPXVs, especially MPXV. A variety of antiviral agents against MPXV and other OPXVs are discussed according to their distinct mechanisms of action: 1) blocking viral entry and fusion, 2) inhibiting DNA replication and processing, 3) disrupting transcription and mRNA processing, 4) preventing virion assembly, maturation and release, 5) modulating immune responses, and 6) mechanism unknown. Overall, this article provides a systematic review of current research progress on potential therapeutic targets and agent for MPXV, aiming to offer innovative insights and strategies for the development of effective therapeutic agents against mpox.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review focuses on the life cycle of the monkeypox virus (MPXV) and the therapeutic agents available for its treatment. The central question is to provide a systematic summary of current research progress on potential therapeutic targets and agents for MPXV, aiming to offer innovative insights and strategies for the development of effective therapeutic agents against mpox. The topic boundary includes MPXV clades and mpox disease, with related viruses such as variola virus, smallpox, cowpox virus, vaccinia virus, molluscum contagiosum virus, and camelpox virus considered supplementary unless material target-specific evidence is also present.
Evidence base and method
The review systematically summarizes the genome, structure, and critical stages in the life cycle of orthopoxviruses (OPXVs), especially MPXV. It discusses a variety of antiviral agents against MPXV and other OPXVs according to their distinct mechanisms of action, including blocking viral entry and fusion, inhibiting DNA replication and processing, disrupting transcription and mRNA processing, preventing virion assembly, maturation and release, modulating immune responses, and mechanisms unknown. The review does not explicitly report on the meta-analysis or consensus process.
Consensus and conclusions
The review highlights that the genome and structure of MPXV are similar to those of other orthopoxviruses (OPXVs), suggesting that strategies used for other OPXVs may be applicable to MPXV. It also states that only tecovirimat has been approved by the European Medicines Agency (EMA) for the treatment of MPXV infection.
Controversies and gaps
The review notes that while the genome and structure of MPXV are similar to those of other OPXVs, the applicability of strategies used for other OPXVs to MPXV remains an area of uncertainty. Additionally, the review does not provide specific details on the mechanisms of action of the various antiviral agents discussed, leaving some evidence gaps in understanding their efficacy and potential for use against MPXV.
Research and practice implications
The review suggests that further research is needed to explore the potential of antiviral agents against MPXV, particularly those with mechanisms of action that are not yet fully understood. It also emphasizes the importance of developing new therapeutic agents for MPXV, given the limited options currently available. The article aims to provide a foundation for future research and development efforts in this area.

刚果民主共和国冲突和医疗崩溃的双重负担:呼吁采取行动。

Dual Burden of Conflicts and Healthcare Collapse in the Democratic Republic of Congo: A Call for Action.
作者： Emile CYUBAHIRO, Darius BENIMANA
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该审查重点关注刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 持续冲突和医疗保健系统崩溃对公共卫生的影响,特别强调了对 猴痘病毒 相关疾病 暴发 和医疗保健获取的复合影响。核心问题是指导针对具体情况的干预措施和政策行动,以实现可持续复苏。

Dual Burden of Conflicts and Healthcare Collapse in the Democratic Republic of Congo: A Call for Action.

Authors: Emile CYUBAHIRO, Darius BENIMANA
Original abstract (English)
ABSTRACT Background Healthcare is protected under international humanitarian law through principles such as civilian–combatant distinction and necessity. However, rising attacks on medical personnel and facilities in the Democratic Republic of Congo (DRC) represent serious violations of these laws, threatening uninterrupted care and undermining basic human rights. Objective This narrative review synthesizes literature to highlight the compounded public health impact of ongoing conflict and health system collapse in the DRC to guide context‐sensitive interventions and policy actions for sustainable recovery. Methods This narrative review synthesizes data up to 2025 from peer‐reviewed journals, accessed through PubMed, Google Scholar, World Health Organization (WHO), and World Bank databases, and other sources. Results At the time of this study, ongoing conflict in the DRC, especially in the eastern provinces, has severely undermined healthcare access. Armed violence has led to the complete destruction of over 70 health facilities in North Kivu and partial destruction of 15 facilities in South Kivu, displacement of 7.3 million people, and disrupted vaccination campaigns, worsening cholera, Mpox, malaria, and measles outbreaks. Maternal and child mortality remain high due to insecurity, destroyed maternity centers, and limited antenatal care. Over 25% of the population faces severe food insecurity, with over 2 million children acutely malnourished. Insecurity, aid worker attacks, poor infrastructure, corruption, weak coordination, and donor fatigue hinder sustained recovery. Conclusion The DRC faces a critical health crisis driven by conflict and healthcare system collapse. Urgent global action is required to protect health services, support frontline workers, and restore peace. Although some humanitarian responses have been implemented, sustainable, coordinated, context‐sensitive interventions and long‐term investments remain essential to uphold human rights and prevent further humanitarian catastrophe.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on the impact of ongoing conflict and healthcare system collapse in the Democratic Republic of Congo (DRC) on public health, particularly highlighting the compounded effects on mpox virus-related disease outbreaks and healthcare access. The central question is to guide context-sensitive interventions and policy actions for sustainable recovery.
Evidence base and method
This narrative review synthesizes data up to 2025 from peer-reviewed journals, accessed through PubMed, Google Scholar, World Health Organization (WHO), and World Bank databases, and other sources. The review method is based on synthesizing existing literature rather than conducting new experiments or analyses.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that the DRC faces a critical health crisis driven by conflict and healthcare system collapse, with ongoing conflict in the eastern provinces severely undermining healthcare access. The compounded public health impact includes disrupted vaccination campaigns and worsened outbreaks of cholera, Mpox, malaria, and measles.
Controversies and gaps
The review highlights that insecurity, aid worker attacks, poor infrastructure, corruption, weak coordination, and donor fatigue hinder sustained recovery. There is a lack of detailed, target-specific evidence on the epidemiology and transmission dynamics of the mpox virus in the context of conflict.
Research and practice implications
Urgent global action is required to protect health services, support frontline workers, and restore peace. Sustainable, coordinated, context-sensitive interventions and long-term investments remain essential to uphold human rights and prevent further humanitarian catastrophe.

通过战略性 疫苗 沟通重建公众信任:近期全球卫生紧急事件的经验教训和未来大流行病防范框架

Rebuilding Public Trust Through Strategic Vaccine Communication: Lessons from Recent Global Health Emergencies and a Framework for Future Pandemic Preparedness
作者： Olukunle O. Akanbi, Chisom Olivia Emenuga, Uchenna Onyekachi Uchejuru, Ziyadat Temitope Ekundayo, Emmanuel Udochukwu, Edna Beverla, Amosa Sulyman Olayinka, K. Akanbi, Onyekachi Uchenna Uchejuru
摘要中文翻译有效的 疫苗 沟通已成为全球卫生应急准备的一个决定性挑战,因为 疫苗 开发中的科学进步并未伴随着公众信任度的相应提升。最近 暴发(特别是埃博拉病毒、COVID-19 和 猴痘)的经验表明,当沟通不一致、错误信息激增以及公众对卫生机构的信心减弱时,仅靠 疫苗 的可用性不足以实现 疫苗 的高采用率。这篇叙述性评论批判性地综合了当前的证据,以检验公众信任的决定因素,评估现有 疫苗 传播策略的优势和局限性,并确定未来研究和政策的优先事项。该审查强调了有效 疫苗 沟通的五个相互关联的决定因素:机构透明度、基于证据的消息传递、值得信赖的社区伙伴关系、数字信息监控和持续的沟通适应。它还进一步确定了持续存在的挑战,包括反应性沟通方式、政治两极分化、获取可信信息的不平等以及人工智能驱动的错误信息日益增长的影响。目前的证据还受到横断面研究占主导地位、低收入和中等收入国家代表性不足以及对长期信任建设干预措施评估不足的限制。为了应对这些挑战,我们提出了自适应 疫苗 信任通信(AVTC)框架,该框架将 疫苗 通信概念化为一种连续的自适应过程,而不是单向的信息传输。通过整合机构透明度、基于证据的信息传递、值得信赖的社区伙伴关系、数字信息监测和持续反馈,该框架为加强公众信任、提高 疫苗 信心并加强对未来全球卫生紧急情况的准备提供了实用的路线图。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
中心问题研究了战略性 疫苗 沟通如何在全球突发卫生事件期间影响公众信任,重点关注信任的决定因素、现有战略的优势和局限性,以及 疫苗 可预防疾病背景下未来研究和政策的优先事项。
证据基础与方法
该综述是一种叙述性综合,评估有关 疫苗 沟通策略、机构透明度、基于证据的消息传递、可信伙伴关系、数字监控和适应性框架的证据,但不报告系统检索日期、资格标准或纳入研究计数。
共识与结论
该审查确定了有效 疫苗 通信的五个相互关联的决定因素:机构透明度、基于证据的消息传递、可信社区伙伴关系、数字信息监控和持续通信适应,并提出自适应 疫苗 信任通信 (AVTC) 框架作为实用路线图。
争议与缺口
持续存在的挑战包括反应性沟通方式、政治两极分化、获取可信信息的不平等、人工智能驱动的错误信息影响力日益增大,以及横断面研究占主导地位、低收入和中等收入国家代表性不足以及对长期信任建设干预措施评估不足等限制。
科研与实践启示
优先事项包括开发适应性的、基于证据的沟通框架,将透明度、社区伙伴关系和数字监测结合起来,并解决获取可信信息方面的不平等问题,以在未来的卫生紧急情况下加强公众的信任和信心。

Rebuilding Public Trust Through Strategic Vaccine Communication: Lessons from Recent Global Health Emergencies and a Framework for Future Pandemic Preparedness

Authors: Olukunle O. Akanbi, Chisom Olivia Emenuga, Uchenna Onyekachi Uchejuru, Ziyadat Temitope Ekundayo, Emmanuel Udochukwu, Edna Beverla, Amosa Sulyman Olayinka, K. Akanbi, Onyekachi Uchenna Uchejuru
Original abstract (English)
Effective vaccine communication has become a defining challenge of global health emergency preparedness, as scientific advances in vaccine development have not been accompanied by comparable gains in public trust. Experiences from recent outbreaks, particularly Ebola, COVID-19, and mpox, demonstrate that vaccine availability alone is insufficient to achieve high vaccine uptake when communication is inconsistent, misinformation proliferates, and public confidence in health institutions is weakened. This narrative review critically synthesizes current evidence to examine the determinants of public trust, evaluate the strengths and limitations of existing vaccine communication strategies, and identify priorities for future research and policy. The review highlights five interconnected determinants of effective vaccine communication: institutional transparency, evidence-based messaging, trusted community partnerships, digital information monitoring, and continuous communication adaptation. It further identifies persistent challenges, including reactive communication approaches, political polarization, inequitable access to trustworthy information, and the growing influence of artificial intelligence-driven misinformation. Current evidence is also limited by the predominance of cross-sectional studies, underrepresentation of low- and middle-income countries, and insufficient evaluation of long-term trust-building interventions. To address these challenges, we propose the Adaptive Vaccine Trust Communication (AVTC) Framework, which conceptualizes vaccine communication as a continuous adaptive process rather than a one-way transfer of information. By integrating institutional transparency, evidence-based messaging, trusted community partnerships, digital information monitoring, and continuous feedback, the framework provides a practical roadmap for strengthening public trust, improving vaccine confidence, and enhancing preparedness for future global health emergencies.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The central question examines how strategic vaccine communication influences public trust during global health emergencies, focusing on determinants of trust, strengths and limitations of existing strategies, and priorities for future research and policy in the context of vaccine-preventable diseases.
Evidence base and method
The review is a narrative synthesis that evaluates evidence on vaccine communication strategies, institutional transparency, evidence-based messaging, trusted partnerships, digital monitoring, and adaptive frameworks, without reporting systematic search dates, eligibility criteria, or included-study counts.
Consensus and conclusions
The review identifies five interconnected determinants of effective vaccine communication: institutional transparency, evidence-based messaging, trusted community partnerships, digital information monitoring, and continuous communication adaptation, and proposes the Adaptive Vaccine Trust Communication (AVTC) Framework as a practical roadmap.
Controversies and gaps
Persistent challenges include reactive communication approaches, political polarization, inequitable access to trustworthy information, and the growing influence of artificial intelligence-driven misinformation, alongside limitations such as predominance of cross-sectional studies, underrepresentation of low- and middle-income countries, and insufficient evaluation of long-term trust-building interventions.
Research and practice implications
Priorities include developing adaptive, evidence-based communication frameworks that integrate transparency, community partnerships, and digital monitoring, and addressing inequities in access to trustworthy information to strengthen public trust and vaccine confidence during future health emergencies.

家庭和医疗机构中 MPXV 的环境表面和空气污染:检测和可行性的系统评价

Environmental surface and air contamination of MPXV in household and healthcare settings: a systematic review of detection and viability
作者： Efe SEMERCİOĞLU, Sena Birsen Guclu, Zeynep Umay Gürbüz, Zeynep Yagmur Yilmaz, İrem Yaşar, Gaye Erkek, Defne Beybolat, Berça Elif Kaya, Şiran Keske, Önder ERGÖNÜL
摘要中文翻译猴痘病毒 (MPXV) 传播 主要通过直接接触受感染的皮肤损伤或体液发生。然而,2022 年和 2024 年全球 暴发 再次引发了人们对受污染表面和空气对 传播 动态的潜在影响的担忧。本系统综述旨在综合有关家庭和医疗机构中 MPXV 环境表面和空气污染的最新证据。对文献进行了系统检索,以确定报告临床或家庭环境中 MPXV 环境表面或空气采样的研究。提取了有关采样方法、时间、检测指标和病毒培养结果的数据。将表面分类为功能组以进行交叉研究比较,并在样本水平数据可用时计算中位 Ct 值。还对医护人员 (HCW) 的暴露和感染结果进行了描述性分析。二十三项研究符合纳入标准。在具有可分析分子数据的 391 个直接与患者接触的表面中,73.1% 的 MPXV DNA PCR 呈阳性。在报告 Ct 值的样本中,中位 Ct 为 32.2(范围:16.1-39.0),而定量病毒载量范围为 0.301 至 5.38 log10 拷贝/cm2。与无孔表面相比,多孔材料,特别是床上用品和软表面,表现出较低的中位 Ct 值。尽管病毒载量普遍较低,但空气采样显示超过一半的合格样本呈 PCR 阳性。在一部分研究中进行了病毒培养,在少数样品中回收了活病毒,主要来自 Ct 值较低的表面。在 543 名暴露的医护人员中,只有 4 人在没有直接接触或锐器伤害的情况下被感染,尽管有记录显示在一些环境中存在环境污染。大多数研究在随访期间没有发生额外的医护人员感染。在家庭和医疗机构中,经常可以检测到 MPXV DNA 造成的环境污染。然而,病毒 DNA 的检测并不总是与病毒活力相关,并且在医护人员中确认的职业 传播 仍然很少。目前的证据支持直接接触 传播 的主导地位,而环境污染可能代表特定条件下的背景放大因素。需要进一步研究结合标准化采样方法和基于培养的活力评估,以更好地确定环境 MPXV 污染的临床相关性。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述检查了家庭和医疗机构中猴痘病毒 (MPXV) 的环境表面和空气污染,重点关注受污染的表面和空气是否会影响 MPXV 传播 动态,而不仅仅是直接接触受感染的皮肤损伤或体液。
证据基础与方法
本系统综述检索了报告临床或家庭环境中 MPXV 环境表面或空气采样的研究,提取了有关采样方法、时间、检测指标和病毒培养结果的数据。23 项研究符合纳入标准,对 391 个表面和空气样本进行了基于分子和培养的分析。
共识与结论
MPXV DNA 环境污染在家庭和医疗机构中经常可检测到,391 个患者直接接触表面中有 73.1% 呈 PCR 阳性,Ct 中位数为 32.2(范围:16.1-39.0)。多孔材料(如垫料)的 Ct 值中值低于无孔表面。空气采样显示,超过一半的合格样本呈 PCR 阳性,但病毒载量普遍较低。在少数样品中发现了活病毒,主要来自 Ct 值较低的表面。
争议与缺口
MPXV DNA 的检测并不总是与病毒活力相关,尽管有环境污染记录,但在医护人员中确认的职业 传播 仍然很少见。该综述强调了采样方法的异质性以及采用标准化方法来评估环境污染的临床相关性的必要性。
科研与实践启示
需要结合标准化采样方法和基于培养的活力评估的进一步研究来确定环境 MPXV 污染的临床相关性。在高风险环境中加强 监测 和感染控制措施可能是必要的,特别是在直接接触 传播 不太明显的情况下。

Environmental surface and air contamination of MPXV in household and healthcare settings: a systematic review of detection and viability

Authors: Efe SEMERCİOĞLU, Sena Birsen Guclu, Zeynep Umay Gürbüz, Zeynep Yagmur Yilmaz, İrem Yaşar, Gaye Erkek, Defne Beybolat, Berça Elif Kaya, Şiran Keske, Önder ERGÖNÜL
Original abstract (English)
Mpox virus (MPXV) transmission primarily occurs through direct contact with infected skin lesions or body fluids. However, the 2022 and 2024 global outbreaks renewed concerns regarding the potential contribution of contaminated surfaces and air to transmission dynamics. This systematic review aimed to synthesize current evidence on environmental surface and air contamination of MPXV in household and healthcare settings. A systematic search of the literature was conducted to identify studies reporting environmental surface or air sampling for MPXV in clinical or household settings. Data were extracted on sampling methods, timing, detection metrics, and viral culture results. Surfaces were categorized into functional groups to allow cross-study comparison, and median Ct values were calculated when sample-level data were available. Healthcare worker (HCW) exposure and infection outcomes were also descriptively analysed. Twenty-three studies met the inclusion criteria. Across 391 direct patient-contact surfaces with analysable molecular data, 73.1% were PCR-positive for MPXV DNA. Among samples reporting Ct values, the median Ct was 32.2 (range: 16.1–39.0), while quantitative viral loads ranged from 0.301 to 5.38 log10 copies/cm2. Porous materials, particularly bedding and soft surfaces, demonstrated lower median Ct values compared to non-porous surfaces. Air sampling showed PCR positivity in over half of eligible samples, although viral loads were generally low. Viral culture was performed in a subset of studies, with viable virus recovered in a minority of samples, predominantly from surfaces with lower Ct values. Among 543 exposed healthcare workers, only 4 became infected without direct contact or sharps injury, despite documented environmental contamination in several settings. No additional HCW infections occurred during follow-up in most studies. Environmental contamination with MPXV DNA is frequently detectable in both household and healthcare settings. However, detection of viral DNA does not consistently correlate with viral viability, and confirmed occupational transmission among healthcare workers remains rare. Current evidence supports the predominance of direct contact transmission, while environmental contamination may represent a contextual amplification factor under specific conditions. Further studies incorporating standardized sampling methods and culture-based viability assessments are needed to better define the clinical relevance of environmental MPXV contamination.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines environmental surface and air contamination of Monkeypox virus (MPXV) in household and healthcare settings, focusing on the central question of whether contaminated surfaces and air contribute to MPXV transmission dynamics beyond direct contact with infected skin lesions or body fluids.
Evidence base and method
This systematic review searched for studies reporting environmental surface or air sampling for MPXV in clinical or household settings, extracting data on sampling methods, timing, detection metrics, and viral culture results. Twenty-three studies met inclusion criteria, with molecular and culture-based analyses conducted across 391 surfaces and air samples.
Consensus and conclusions
Environmental contamination with MPXV DNA is frequently detectable in both household and healthcare settings, with 73.1% of 391 direct patient-contact surfaces PCR-positive and median Ct of 32.2 (range: 16.1–39.0). Porous materials like bedding showed lower median Ct values than non-porous surfaces. Air sampling showed PCR positivity in over half of eligible samples, though viral loads were generally low. Viable virus was recovered in a minority of samples, predominantly from surfaces with lower Ct values.
Controversies and gaps
Detection of MPXV DNA does not consistently correlate with viral viability, and confirmed occupational transmission among healthcare workers remains rare despite documented environmental contamination. The review highlights heterogeneity in sampling methods and the need for standardized approaches to assess the clinical relevance of environmental contamination.
Research and practice implications
Further studies incorporating standardized sampling methods and culture-based viability assessments are needed to define the clinical relevance of environmental MPXV contamination. Enhanced surveillance and infection control measures in high-risk settings may be warranted, particularly where direct contact transmission is less evident.

病例报告:猴痘 感染继发的心肌炎。

A 高优先级公共卫生新闻

美国和世界各地的猴痘:现状 - 疾病控制与预防中心 |疾病预防控制中心 (.gov)

Monkeypox in the United States and Around the World: Current Situation - Centers for Disease Control and Prevention | CDC (.gov)
新闻简报The U.S. Centers for Disease Control and Prevention reported on 11 August 2026 that mpox outbreaks caused by clade I and clade II viruses continue to circulate globally. The clade IIb outbreak that began in 2022 has exceeded 100,000 cases across 122 countries, with fewer than 0.1% of patients dying. In the United States, more than 40 laboratory-confirmed clade I cases have been detected since November 2024, nearly all linked to travel from Central/Eastern Africa or Western Europe or to contacts of travelers; no deaths have been reported among these cases. Recent clade I outbreaks in Central and Eastern Africa have persisted since late 2023, with subclade Ib causing travel-associated cases in multiple regions and very low mortality. CDC reiterated vaccination as a key prevention tool and is expanding domestic surveillance and international support to limit further spread.
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年 8 月 11 日发布;活动数据涵盖 2022-2026 年,持续进行 暴发。
地点与对象
美国;中非和东非;西欧;西非;全球(122 个国家)。
事件
CDC 报告正在进行的 I 型分支和 II 型分支 猴痘 暴发 已确诊病例、与旅行相关的传播和低死亡率;指南强调 疫苗接种 和 监测。
规模、影响与风险
分支 IIb:自 2022 年以来,122 个国家的病例数超过 100,000 例;死亡人数<0.1%。2024 年 11 月以来的病例;与旅行相关的;<0.5%(进化枝 Ib)至约 3%(进化枝 Ia)死亡。居民人数仍然很少。
应对、状态与不确定性
CDC 提供 疫苗接种 建议,提高提供者意识,加强 监测(包括废水),并进行国际合作以阻止传播;数据可能会发生变化。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Monkeypox in the United States and Around the World: Current Situation - Centers for Disease Control and Prevention | CDC (.gov)

News Brief
The U.S. Centers for Disease Control and Prevention reported on 11 August 2026 that mpox outbreaks caused by clade I and clade II viruses continue to circulate globally. The clade IIb outbreak that began in 2022 has exceeded 100,000 cases across 122 countries, with fewer than 0.1% of patients dying. In the United States, more than 40 laboratory-confirmed clade I cases have been detected since November 2024, nearly all linked to travel from Central/Eastern Africa or Western Europe or to contacts of travelers; no deaths have been reported among these cases. Recent clade I outbreaks in Central and Eastern Africa have persisted since late 2023, with subclade Ib causing travel-associated cases in multiple regions and very low mortality. CDC reiterated vaccination as a key prevention tool and is expanding domestic surveillance and international support to limit further spread.

What to know There are two types of the virus that causes monkeypox, clade I and clade II. Both types can be spread, treated, and prevented in the same ways. Each clade has two subclades (clade Ia and clade Ib; clade IIa and IIb). Recent outbreaks of monkeypox have spread initially through intimate or sexual contact. In some cases, monkeypox was then spread within households. There have been ongoing outbreaks of clade I monkeypox in Central and Eastern Africa since late 2023. Several Western European countries are reporting outbreaks that are likely related to the Central and Eastern African outbreaks but are affecting different people. Travel-associated clade I monkeypox cases have almost exclusively been attributed to subclade Ib; there have been no deaths associated with these cases and most people had relatively mild illness. The ongoing global outbreak of clade IIb monkeypox that began in 2022 has caused more than 100,000 cases in 122 total countries, including 115 countries where monkeypox was not previously reported. CDC has vaccination recommendations for people at risk in the United States or who are traveling to countries with clade I outbreaks or new clade II outbreaks. Current situation In the United States Clade I monkeypox The risk posed by the clade I monkeypox outbreak to most people within the United States remains low. Since November 2024, there have been more than 40 laboratory-confirmed cases of clade I monkeypox in the United States. Nearly all were diagnosed in people who: Had recently traveled from areas associated with the outbreak in Central and Eastern Africa; Had recently traveled from parts of Western Europe where newer outbreaks are being reported; OR Were linked to people who had traveled from these areas. We expect additional cases in Europe and the United States. Reminder Vaccination is an important tool in stopping the spread of monkeypox. If you have certain risk factors that make you eligible, you can help protect yourself from monkeypox by getting the 2-dose monkeypox vaccine. Monkeypox Vaccination Clade II monkeypox Clade II monkeypox is still circulating at low levels. Fall 2025 marked the highest monthly case counts since the peak of the outbreak in 2022. Clade I Monkeypox Outbreaks Cases of clade I monkeypox have been diagnosed worldwide. Clade II Monkeypox Outbreaks Clade II monkeypox is spreading in the United States and around the world. Across the globe Clade I monkeypox Outbreaks of clade I monkeypox in Central and Eastern Africa continue. Beginning in Fall 2025, several countries in Western Europe began reporting clade Ib monkeypox cases among individuals who had no documented history of international travel. These cases were likely related to male-to-male sexual contact. Other travel-associated cases of clade I monkeypox have been reported from other parts of Africa, as well as Asia, Europe, the Middle East, North America, South America, and Australia. Recent monkeypox outbreaks have low death rates During recent outbreaks, death rates ranged from than less than 0.5% (clade Ib) to about 3% (clade Ia). Clade II is the type that caused the ongoing global outbreak that began in 2022. Fewer than 0.1% of people infected with clade II die from the disease. Clade II monkeypox Several countries in West Africa have been experiencing an outbreak of clade II monkeypox since summer 2025. Travelers to Liberia and Ghana and should review the Travel Health Notice, which includes information about the monkeypox vaccine for eligible people. Global monkeypox case data For global case data for clade I and clade II monkeypox since January 1, 2024, see Global Mpox Trends. Confirmed cases include those that are laboratory confirmed as monkeypox virus and may include cases only confirmed as orthopoxvirus. These data are provided for situational awareness and are subject to change. What CDC is doing CDC raises awareness for U.S. healthcare providers, including the latest guidance for diagnosing monkeypox and clinical decision-making. CDC continues critical work to protect the United States from emerging and infectious diseases. CDC has collaborated closely with key partners in affected countries to help support efforts to stop monkeypox at the source. CDC works closely with U.S. state, tribal, local, and territorial public health departments to provide recommendations for clinical management, diagnosis, and prevention of domestic monkeypox cases. in the U.S. CDC continually increases capacity in communities across the United States for early detection of monkeypox through existing surveillance systems, including wastewater testing. CDC raises awareness for healthcare providers, including the latest guidance for diagnosing monkeypox and clinical decision-making. CDC collaborated with governmental and civil society partners in affected countries to collect and analyze case data, and to identify how monkeypox is spreading. CDC coordinated technical assistance in response to urgent in-country needs, including: increasing the number of monkeypox testing sites; improving specimen transport networks to quickly identify new cases; assisting with Ministry of Health-led vaccine implementation; strengthening emergency management systems; improving case surveillance; and training healthcare workers. CDC's staff throughout Africa provide critical, real-time information to inform U.S. preparedness efforts. In the United States CDC works closely with state, tribal, local, and territorial public health departments to provide recommendations for clinical management, diagnosis, and prevention of monkeypox cases in the U.S. CDC continually increases capacity in communities across the United States for early detection of monkeypox through existing surveillance systems, including wastewater testing. CDC raises awareness for healthcare providers, including the latest guidance for diagnosing monkeypox and clinical decision-making. CDC has information and recommendations for members of the public, including those traveling to Central or Eastern Africa: Travel Health Notice and Health Alert Network advisory. CDC continues to work with international partners to help reduce monkeypox transmission. I 型猴痘 暴发 - 疾病控制和预防中心 |疾病预防控制中心 (.gov)

Clade I Monkeypox Outbreaks - Centers for Disease Control and Prevention | CDC (.gov)
新闻简报美国疾病控制和预防中心 (CDC) 报告称,自 2023 年底以来,中非和东非持续出现 I 型猴痘 暴发,自 2024 年 1 月以来,全球确诊病例超过 55,000 例,其中 150 多人死亡。较新的 暴发 已在西欧国家和美国出现,截至 2026 年 7 月,美国报告了 40 多例病例。传播 通过性接触、家庭互动和动物接触而发生。在美国,大多数人的风险较低,但男男性行为者的风险为中等。Ib 分支一直是推动传播的主要分支,高收入国家没有死亡报告。CDC 正在积极监测和应对这些 暴发,为病例管理和预防提供指导。这些数据可能会发生变化,旨在用于态势感知。
查看新闻五要素
时间
I 支猴痘 暴发 自 2023 年底以来一直持续存在,自 2026 年 3 月以来在美国的发病频率有所增加。截至 2026 年 7 月,美国报告了 40 多例病例,截至 2026 年 4 月,刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 仍然是 Ib 支支病例最多的国家。疾病预防控制中心预计欧洲和美国将出现更多病例。
地点与对象
暴发 事件发生在中非和东非,特别是刚果民主共和国,并已蔓延至西欧国家和美国。曾到过 暴发 地区或与去过 暴发 地区的人有关联的人中已有病例报告。受影响的人群包括最近去过中非/东非或西欧的个人,美国大多数人的风险较低,但男男性行为者的风险为低至中度。
事件
自 2023 年底以来,中非和东非已报道了 I 支猴痘的 暴发,西欧和美国也报告了较新的 暴发。疾病预防控制中心已确认这些 暴发 并正在监测其传播情况。暴发 的特征是通过性接触、家庭接触和接触受感染的动物来表现 传播。
规模、影响与风险
自2024年1月以来,全球报告了超过55,000例I型猴痘确诊病例,其中150多人死亡。截至 2026 年 7 月,美国报告了 40 多例病例,但尚未造成死亡。美国 Ib 分支病例中大多数人面临的风险已导致中非和东非以及世界其他地区的大部分传播,迄今为止,高收入国家尚未报告死亡病例。
应对、状态与不确定性
CDC 正在积极支持刚果民主共和国的研究和应对工作,包括疾病 监测、建设当地实验室能力、加强公共卫生和实验室人力、病例调查、病例管理、感染预防和控制、边境卫生和风险沟通。数据可能会发生变化,并用于态势感知。疾病预防控制中心 (CDC) 为美国的报告、检测和病例管理提供临床和公共卫生指南。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Clade I Monkeypox Outbreaks - Centers for Disease Control and Prevention | CDC (.gov)

News Brief
The CDC reports ongoing outbreaks of clade I monkeypox in Central and Eastern Africa since late 2023, with global confirmed cases exceeding 55,000 since January 2024, including over 150 deaths. Newer outbreaks have emerged in Western European countries and the United States, with more than 40 cases reported in the U.S. as of July 2026. Transmission occurs through sexual contact, household interactions, and animal exposure. The risk to most people in the U.S. is low, though it is moderate for men who have sex with men. Clade Ib has been the dominant subclade driving spread, with no deaths reported in high-income countries. The CDC is actively monitoring and responding to these outbreaks, providing guidance for case management and prevention. The data are subject to change and are intended for situational awareness.

Monkeypox Explore This Topic Search Search Clear Search For Everyone Current Situation U.S. Case Data About Signs and Symptoms Outbreaks How It Spreads Prevention Vaccines View all Health Care Providers Clinical Overview Clinical Signs and Symptoms Vaccine for Monkeypox Prevention in the U.S. Clinical Testing and Diagnosis Clinical Care Infection Control Guidance Veterinary Guidance View all Public Health Case Definitions Community Monitoring & Risk Assessment Guidance for Health Departments Laboratory Information Communications Resources View all search clear search Monkeypox Menu clear search For Everyone Current Situation U.S. Case Data About Signs and Symptoms Outbreaks How It Spreads Prevention Vaccines View All Home Health Care Providers Clinical Overview Clinical Signs and Symptoms Vaccine for Monkeypox Prevention in the U.S. Clinical Testing and Diagnosis Clinical Care Infection Control Guidance Veterinary Guidance View All Public Health Case Definitions Community Monitoring & Risk Assessment Guidance for Health Departments Laboratory Information Communications Resources View All Current Situation U.S. Case Data About Signs and Symptoms Outbreaks How It Spreads Prevention Vaccines View Menu Clade I Monkeypox Outbreaks For Everyone Aug. 11, 2026, visit link for details. What to know There have been outbreaks of clade I monkeypox in Central and Eastern Africa since late 2023. Since January 2024, more than 55,000 confirmed cases of clade I monkeypox have been reported globally, including more than 150 deaths. Newer outbreaks in several Western European countries are likely related to this ongoing outbreak. Transmission dynamics are changing. Only a small proportion of exported clade I monkeypox cases worldwide this year have originated in Central or Eastern African countries. Recent travel-associated clade I monkeypox cases have almost exclusively been attributed to subclade Ib; there have been no deaths associated with these cases and available data have detailed relatively mild disease courses. Outbreak overview In the United States The risk posed by the clade I monkeypox outbreak to most people within the United States remains low. The risk to men who have sex with men is low to moderate. Clade I cases in the United States have increased in frequency since March 2026. As of July 2026, there have been more than 40 clade I monkeypox cases reported in the United States. Nearly all U.S. cases were diagnosed in people who: Had recently traveled to areas associated with the outbreak in Central and Eastern Africa; Had recently traveled to parts of Western Europe where newer outbreaks are being reported; OR Were linked to people who had traveled from these areas. CDC expects additional cases in Europe and the United States. Preventing Monkeypox While Traveling Learn where monkeypox is spreading and how to lower your risk of getting it during travel. In Central and Eastern Africa Clade Ia monkeypox outbreaks began impacting western areas of the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) in 2024. Available data indicate that clade Ia monkeypox has spread through sexual contact, regular household contact, and encounters with infected dead or live wild animals. Clade Ib monkeypox cases initially impacted eastern areas of DRC in late 2023. This subclade has driven much of the spread throughout Central and Eastern Africa and most travel-associated cases to other parts of the world. Available data indicate that these cases at first spread through heterosexual intimate or sexual contact between adults, then spread likely occurred within households, including to children. The disease spread in 2024 and 2025 to neighboring countries of DRC, only some of which are endemic for the virus that causes monkeypox, and sustained local spread occurred. As of April 2026, the Democratic Republic of the Congo is still the country with the majority of clade Ib cases. Outside Central and Eastern Africa Several countries in other parts of Africa, Asia, Europe, North America, and South America, as well as Australia, have confirmed clade I monkeypox cases mostly in travelers who'd recently been in areas with clade I outbreaks. To date, there have been more than 300 clade Ib cases in high-income countries, and no deaths have been reported. Beginning in Fall 2025, several countries in Western Europe began reporting clade Ib monkeypox cases among individuals who had no documented history of international travel. These cases were likely related to intimate or sexual exposure among men who have sex with men. We expect additional cases in Europe and the United States. For data since January 1, 2024, see 2022-24 Monkeypox (Monkeypox) Outbreak: Global Trends (shinyapps.io). Confirmed cases include those that are laboratory confirmed as monkeypox virus and may include cases only confirmed as orthopoxvirus. These data are provided for situational awareness and are subject to change. Clade I monkeypox and children Early in the Central and Eastern African outbreak, a high proportion of suspected cases in some areas were reported in children younger than 15 years of age. Historically, cases of monkeypox in children where monkeypox occurs regularly often happen after contact with wild animals that carry the disease. People then can spread the disease to close contacts, including in their households. The number of children with suspected monkeypox in this outbreak is probably due to spread within and between close households, the large number of younger people in affected countries, misdiagnosis of other diseases (such as measles), poor sanitary conditions, limited access to health services, and a high level of food insecurity and malnutrition. Based on what we know today, we don't expect to see the same sort of pattern in the United States for several reasons, including smaller household sizes, access to cleaning and disinfecting products, and improved access to medical care, equipment, and information about preventing spread while caring for patients. Notably, there are no consistent reports of schools as drivers of monkeypox transmission during any recent outbreaks. If cases of clade I monkeypox do happen in children the United States, CDC has clinical and public health guidance for reporting, testing, and effective case management. What CDC is doing CDC has been supporting DRC monkeypox research and response for more than 20 years. CDC and other U.S. government agencies have continued working in-country with partners in affected areas on disease surveillance, building local laboratory capacity, strengthening public health and laboratory workforce capacity, case investigation, case management, infection prevention and control, border health, and risk communication and community engagement. CDC staff brought laboratory supplies and trained local staff on laboratory equipment Resources Monkeypox in the United States and Around the World: Current Situation There are outbreaks of clade I and clade II monkeypox around the world. View five news elements
Time
The outbreaks of clade I monkeypox have been ongoing since late 2023, with increased frequency in the United States since March 2026. As of July 2026, more than 40 cases were reported in the U.S., and as of April 2026, the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) remains the country with the majority of clade Ib cases. The CDC expects additional cases in Europe and the United States.
Location and population
The outbreaks have occurred in Central and Eastern Africa, particularly in the DRC, and have spread to Western European countries and the United States. Cases have been reported in people who traveled to outbreak areas or were linked to those who had traveled. population affected includes individuals with recent travel to Central/Eastern Africa or Western Europe, and the risk to most people in the U.S. is low, though it is low to moderate for men who have sex with men.
Event
Outbreaks of clade I monkeypox have been reported in Central and Eastern Africa since late 2023, with newer outbreaks in Western Europe and the U.S. The CDC has confirmed these outbreaks and is monitoring their spread. The outbreaks are characterized by transmission through sexual contact, household contact, and encounters with infected animals.
Scale, impact and risk
Since January 2024, more than 55,000 confirmed cases of clade I monkeypox have been reported globally, with over 150 deaths. In the U.S., as of July 2026, more than 40 cases were reported, and no deaths have been associated with these cases. The risk to most people in the U.S. Clade Ib cases have driven much of the spread in Central and Eastern Africa and to other parts of the world, with no deaths reported in high-income countries to date.
Response, status and uncertainty
The CDC is actively supporting research and response efforts in the DRC, including disease surveillance, building local laboratory capacity, strengthening public health and laboratory workforce, case investigation, case management, infection prevention and control, border health, and risk communication. The data are subject to change and provided for situational awareness. The CDC has clinical and public health guidance for reporting, testing, and case management in the U.S.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Mpox hospitalisations highlight disparities as case patterns shift in Europe - The Brussels Times

News Brief
European mpox surveillance data released on 14 August 2026 show clade I cases holding steady while clade II infections continue to fall across the EU/EEA. Over the past year, 1,083 clade I and 888 clade II cases were reported, with 89–91% occurring among men who have sex with men. Hospitalisation rates reached 12% for clade I and 8% for clade II, and two clade II deaths were recorded, while no clade I fatalities were reported. Vaccination coverage with two doses stood at 22% for clade I and 20% for clade II cases. The European Centre for Disease Prevention and Control cautioned that trend analysis is constrained by incomplete clade typing and potential data revisions.
Original source text (English)
Mpox hospitalisations highlight disparities as case patterns shift in Europe Friday, 14 August 2026 By The Brussels Times Newsroom Credit: Unsplash Mpox cases in the EU/EEA held broadly steady in July, with 153 infections linked to clade I of the virus reported by 12 countries. This is according to new surveillance figures show from the European Centre for Disease Prevention and Control (ECDC) released on Friday. Portugal reported the largest number of clade I cases in July, with 68, while France recorded the most clade II cases, with seven. Overall clade I case numbers changed little month to month, with 159 cases reported in June and 162 in May. Clade II infections continued to fall, with 12 cases reported in July compared with 28 in June and 38 in May. Across the past 12 months (August 2025 to July 2026), EU/EEA countries reported 1,083 clade I cases and 888 clade II cases, with 20 countries reporting clade I and 19 reporting clade II. Most cases with complete information were reported among men who have sex with men — 89% of clade I cases and 91% of clade II cases. Hospitalisations and deaths Hospital admission was reported for 12% of clade I cases with complete information and 8% of clade II cases with complete information, the ECDC said. Two deaths were reported among people infected with clade II in the past 12 months, while no deaths were reported among people infected with clade I. Among cases with known vaccination status, 22% of clade I cases and 20% of clade II cases had received two vaccine doses. The ECDC said the number of clade I cases “remains relatively stable” in the EU/EEA, while clade II cases “continue to decline”. Short-term trend analysis is limited because clade information is missing for a substantial share of reported cases and some case details may be updated later, the agency added. B routine priorityPublic-health news

NYC reports rise in Mpox Clade I cases among travelers returning from overseas - AOL.com

News Brief
New York City health authorities reported a rise in mpox clade I cases among travelers returning from Europe, with 14 cases detected in the city over the past month. The first clade I case in NYC was identified in March 2026. The NYC Department of Health and Mental Hygiene urged travelers to complete the two-dose JYNNEOS vaccine series before visiting Europe, noting that prior infection confers immunity and no booster is advised. Transmission occurs through close contact, including sexual contact, and symptoms typically include a painful rash, fever, and swollen lymph nodes. While anyone can contract mpox, global data indicate higher incidence among gay, bisexual, and men who have sex with men.
Original source text (English)
Matthew Euzarraga Updated Sat, August 15, 2026 at 12:19 AM UTC 0 Editor's note: The video above aired in a previous newscast. NEW YORK (PIX11) – New York City is seeing an uptick in cases of an infectious disease that causes a painful rash, known as mpox clade I, especially among those returning from overseas travel in Europe, according to the NYC Department of Mental Hygiene. “We have seen recent cases of clade I mpox among people returning from travel to Europe,” read a social post from the agency posted on Friday afternoon. “If you're traveling to Europe and may be sexually active with new partners while there, make sure to get both doses of the JYNNEOS mpox vaccine before you go.” Discover more local reporting on our homepage The first known case of mpox clade I in the city was reported in March. An individual who tested positive for the virus had traveled internationally, according to NYC health officials. Within the last month, 14 cases of Mpox have been reported in the city, according to data What is Mpox? Formerly known as monkeypox, mpox is a viral illness caused by the monkeypox virus. Common symptoms of mpox are a skin rash or lesions, which can last two to four weeks. Fever, headache, muscle aches, back pain, low energy, and swollen lymph nodes are also common, according to the World Health Organization . Mpox spreads from person to person through close contact with someone who has mpox, including household members. Close contact includes skin-to-skin, kissing, and sexual contact, according to the WHO. Though anyone can test positive for mpox, globally the disease has affected primarily gay, bisexual, and men who have sex with men, according to the World Health Organization . Mpox Vaccination The NYC Health Department recommends completing the two-dose JYNNEOS series . If you’ve already received the first dose, it is never too late to get the second, health officials noted. If you have already been vaccinated, there is no recommendation to receive a booster, according to current health guidance. If you have already tested positive for mpox, you should already be immune, according to health officials. For more information on mpox, click here. 世卫组织建议不要针对猴痘进行大规模 疫苗接种 - 坦帕湾 28

WHO recommends against mass vaccinations for monkeypox - Tampa Bay 28
新闻简报世界卫生组织于 2022 年 6 月 14 日建议反对大规模 猴痘 疫苗接种,而是支持在四天内对高危人群或已知暴露人群进行有针对性的暴露后免疫接种。世卫组织总干事兼顶级猴痘专家强调 疫苗 的供应和临床证据有限,并建议患者和临床医生进行逐案风险效益讨论。截至指导意见,已有 30 多个国家报告了 1,600 例 猴痘 病例,美国疾病预防控制中心 (CDC) 评估公共风险为低风险。典型症状包括皮疹、发烧、不适、头痛和肌肉酸痛。世卫组织还强调了 传播 途径的持续不确定性以及天花免疫力减弱的可能作用。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

WHO recommends against mass vaccinations for monkeypox - Tampa Bay 28

News Brief
The World Health Organization advised on 14 June 2022 against mass mpox vaccinations, instead endorsing targeted post-exposure immunization within four days for people at high risk or with known exposure. WHO’s director-general and top monkeypox expert emphasized limited vaccine supply and clinical evidence, and recommended case-by-case risk-benefit discussions between patients and clinicians. As of the guidance, 1,600 mpox cases had been reported across more than three dozen countries, with the U.S. CDC assessing the public risk as low. Typical symptoms include rash, fever, malaise, headache, and muscle aches. WHO also highlighted ongoing uncertainty about transmission routes and the possible role of waning smallpox immunity.

WHO's top monkeypox expert Dr. Rosamund Lewis said she doesn’t expect the hundreds of cases reported to date to turn into another pandemic, but acknowledged there are still many unknowns about the disease, including how exactly it’s spreading and whether the suspension of mass smallpox immunization decades ago may somehow be speeding its transmission. Goldsmith, Russell Regner/CDC via AP, File) By: Justin Boggs Posted 2:24 PM, Jun 14, 2022 and last updated 2:51 PM, Jun 14, 2022 Despite recent cases of monkeypox throughout the globe, the World Health Organization said Tuesday it is recommending against mass vaccinations for the disease "at this time." The guidance comes as the WHO debates using smallpox vaccines as a way to prevent the spread of monkeypox. "While smallpox vaccines are expected to provide some protection against monkeypox, there is limited clinical data, and limited supply," said Dr. Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus, director-general of the WHO. 1/1 Skip Ad Continue watchingafter the adVisit Advertiser websiteGO TO PAGE Instead, the WHO is recommending targeted vaccinations for those at high risk of contracting the virus and those who have been exposed to it. In guidance issued Tuesday by the WHO, the organization is recommending those who are exposed to the virus should get vaccinated within four days of first exposure to prevent onset of the disease. The WHO recommends vaccines for health workers at risk, laboratory personnel working with orthopoxviruses, clinical laboratory staff performing diagnostic testing for monkeypox and others who may be at risk as per national policy. "Any decision about whether to use vaccines should be made jointly by individuals who may be at risk and their health care provider, based on an assessment of risks and benefits, on a case-by-case basis,” said Tedros. Tedros said there have been 1,600 cases of monkeypox reported worldwide. The virus has been detected in more than three dozen countries, including 32 that are newly affected. The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention said the risk of monkeypox to the public is low but you should avoid contact with others if you develop an unexplained skin rash. Typical symptoms of monkeypox include a rash, fever, malaise, headache and muscle aches. 查看新闻五要素
时间
马达加斯加目前正在与有史以来最大规模的 猴痘 暴发 作斗争,这种情况在过去四个月里一直在持续。分次给药方法于 5 月实施,并持续到 7 月,额外剂量于 7 月底到达。事件日期没有明确说明,但 暴发 被描述为正在进行中。
地点与对象
Madagascar, an African island nation with a population of roughly 33 million people, is experiencing an mpox outbreak. The affected population includes high-risk groups such as sex workers, young children, people living with HIV/AIDS, and medical workers.
事件
马达加斯加正在使用分段给药方式将单个 猴痘 疫苗 分成五份,以在正在进行的 暴发 期间保护更多的人。该方法已获得世界卫生组织在紧急情况下使用的授权,但不作为常规推荐方法。暴发 被描述为马达加斯加有史以来规模最大的一次,迄今为止已记录至少 4,000 例病例。
规模、影响与风险
马达加斯加至少记录了 4,000 例 猴痘 病例,估计有 100 万人面临感染 1b 分支 猴痘 的风险。该病毒通过密切的身体接触传播,包括性接触以及共用毛巾和床单。高危人群包括面临严重并发症的艾滋病毒/艾滋病感染者和同样面临风险的医务人员。
应对、状态与不确定性
到目前为止,马达加斯加已收到由全球免疫联盟和世界卫生组织资助的 60,000 剂 猴痘 疫苗,但这不足以满足需求。公共卫生官员仍然对未来几个月获得更多供应抱有希望。目前正在使用分次给药方法,但它存在风险,例如需要额外的针头和注射器、意外重复使用针头的风险增加以及给药期间的局部反应。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Madagascar forced to divide and share mpox vaccine as epidemic takes off - AOL.com

News Brief
Madagascar is facing its largest-ever mpox outbreak, with at least 4,000 cases recorded so far. Due to critical vaccine shortages, authorities are using fractional dosing, splitting single vaccines into fifths to protect more people. This method, authorized by the WHO in emergencies, is not routinely recommended and carries risks such as the need for additional needles and syringes, increasing the chance of accidental reuse and blood-borne infections like HIV. High-risk groups include sex workers, young children, people living with HIV/AIDS, and medical workers. The outbreak has overwhelmed local hospitals and clinics, and officials hope for more vaccine supplies in the coming months. Mpox spreads through close physical contact, including sexual contact and sharing of personal items, and can lead to severe complications in immunocompromised individuals. The country, the world’s fifth-poorest, has relied on Gavi and the WHO for vaccine funding, but both have faced shortfalls due to reduced global health spending.
Original source text (English)
Authorities in Madagascar are being forced to split single vaccines into fifths to try to protect as many people as possible during an ongoing mpox outbreak. The method, known as “fractional dosing”, is authorised by the World Health Organization (WHO) in emergencies when there are critical vaccine shortages, but is not routinely recommended and carries a level of risk. Madagascar, the African island with a population of roughly 33 million people, is currently battling its largest-ever outbreak of the virus formerly known as monkeypox , with at least 4,000 cases recorded so far. Another one million people are thought to be at risk of contracting clade 1b mpox, a fast-spreading strain of the virus that emerged from the Democratic Republic of the Congo in late 2023. High risk groups include sex workers, young children, and people living with HIV/Aids. Medical workers are also at risk. “Public health authorities in Madagascar have been given too few doses to protect the at-risk population; they were initially given 30,000 doses in May, and a subsequent 30,000 have just arrived at the end of July,” said Akhil Bansal, a doctor of internal medicine working on the mpox response in Madagascar and a policy fellow at the Centre for Global Development . “However, we need to vaccinate more than one million people, so we have been “fractionally dosing”, or splitting vaccines, into fifths, to stretch the supply as far as we can. “This is a somewhat established approach for vaccines, but even then, the number of doses we have is insufficient,” said Dr Bansal. Mpox vaccines can be difficult to procure in countries like Madagascar, which is the world’s fifth-poorest country. - Simon Townsley The WHO has previously endorsed fractional dosing, where vials of vaccine are divided into smaller parts, during health emergencies. The approach – which was used in the UK during Covid – is generally considered a safe and effective way of protecting populations when vaccine supplies are limited. The method was authorised during yellow fever outbreaks in Angola and the Democratic Republic of the Congo, and was also used for mpox vaccines in the US and UK in the summer of 2022, during an outbreak involving a different strain of the virus that primarily affected gay and bisexual men. Several clinical trials have found that, when administered correctly, fractional doses of mpox vaccine can provide protection comparable to full doses. But the approach also carries associated risks. One concern is the need for thousands of extra needles and syringes, increasing the risk of accidental needle re-use and the transmission of blood-borne infections such as HIV. “Madagascar has had to acquire many more needles and syringes because normally one dose is attached to one vaccine,” said Dr Bansal. Fractional doses of a standard vaccine must be administered intradermally – into the top layer of the skin – rather than into the muscle. The skin contains specialised immune cells that can trigger a strong immune response, allowing smaller quantities of vaccine to be used. But intradermal injections are considerably more difficult to administer than injections into the muscle. They require skilled vaccinators to ensure the vaccine is delivered at the correct depth. They are also more likely to cause local reactions, including swelling and itching, and can sometimes leave a distinctive raised “wheel” on the skin – similar to the scars left by the BCG vaccine for tuberculosis which many people received as children before the disease was eradicated in the UK in 1980. Public health officials within Madagascar remain hopeful that they will receive additional vaccine supplies in the coming months, helping to curb an epidemic that has overwhelmed local hospitals and clinics over the past four months. But mpox vaccines are expensive, costing between $50 and $140 per dose, and can be difficult to procure in low-income countries such as Madagascar, which is the world’s fifth-poorest country. The 60,000 doses delivered to the island so far have been funded largely by Gavi, the Vaccine Alliance, and the WHO. Both organisations have faced significant funding shortfalls over the past year as governments, including those of the UK and US, have reduced their spending on global health. Mpox typically causes flu-like symptoms and a painful rash of swollen lesions that can spread across the entire body. For people living with HIV, the virus is particularly dangerous and can lead to severe complications, including brain inflammation, severe bacterial infections of lesions and, in rare cases, can result in death. It spreads mostly through very close physical contact, including sexual contact and through sharing towels and bed sheets. In poor countries, where people often live in closer quarters and have less access to simple infection control measures like soap and clean water, outbreaks can be particularly difficult to control. Protect yourself and your family by learning more about Global Health Security

Time
Madagascar is currently battling its largest-ever mpox outbreak, with the situation ongoing over the past four months. The fractional dosing approach was implemented in May and continued through July, with additional doses arriving at the end of July. The event date is not explicitly stated, but the outbreak is described as ongoing.
Location and population
Madagascar, an African island nation with a population of roughly 33 million people, is experiencing an mpox outbreak. The affected population includes high-risk groups such as sex workers, young children, people living with HIV/AIDS, and medical workers.
Event
Madagascar is using fractional dosing to split single mpox vaccines into fifths to protect more people during an ongoing outbreak. This method is authorized by the WHO in emergencies but is not routinely recommended. The outbreak is described as the largest-ever in Madagascar, with at least 4,000 cases recorded so far.
Scale, impact and risk
At least 4,000 cases of mpox have been recorded in Madagascar, with an estimated one million people at risk of contracting clade 1b mpox. The virus spreads through close physical contact, including sexual contact and sharing of towels and bed sheets. High-risk groups include people living with HIV/AIDS, who face severe complications, and medical workers, who are also at risk.
Response, status and uncertainty
Madagascar has received 60,000 doses of mpox vaccine so far, funded by Gavi and the WHO, but this is insufficient to cover the need. Public health officials remain hopeful for additional supplies in the coming months. The fractional dosing method is being used, but it carries risks such as the need for extra needles and syringes, increased risk of accidental needle reuse, and local reactions during administration.

Damien Center urges mpox vaccination as virus continues to spread - WISH-TV

News Brief
On August 16, 2026, the Damien Center in Indianapolis, Indiana, urged at-risk residents to get vaccinated against mpox as the virus continues to spread. Alan Witchey, the center's president and CEO, emphasized that while the virus is receiving less media attention, the threat remains. The center offers a two-dose intradermal vaccine, which is painless and provides lifetime protection. The doses are spaced four weeks apart, and individuals who missed the second dose can still complete the series. Vaccines are available at no or low cost to those who qualify. The center also warned about online misinformation and encouraged people to consult trusted sources like the CDC or the Damien Center for accurate information. The virus spreads through person-to-person contact, and those at higher risk include people with multiple dating or sexual partners, or those in crowded public spaces with frequent skin-to-skin contact. The Damien Center serves over 13,700 people annually and provides HIV care, primary medical and dental care, food assistance, and housing support. The report does not provide specific case counts, death counts, or exposure numbers, but it highlights the ongoing public health concern and the center's efforts to address it.
Original source text (English)
