This week, Mays spoke with Alan Witchey, president and CEO of the Damien Center. The center is urging at-risk Indianapolis residents to get vaccinated against mpox as the virus continues to spread. Witchey told Mays that while the virus is receiving less media attention, the threat remains. “We want to make sure everybody can get their medications, everyone can get their vaccinations, regardless of ability to pay,” Witchey said. Top Videos The Damien Center offers a two-dose intradermal vaccine. Witchey says the injection is administered just under the skin, is painless, and provides lifetime protection. Vaccination details The two doses are spaced four weeks apart. If you received the first dose but missed the second, you can still complete the series. Vaccines are available at no or low cost to those who qualify. Witchey says the virus spreads through person-to-person contact. Those at higher risk include people with multiple dating or sexual partners, or those in crowded public spaces with frequent skin-to-skin contact. About the Damien Center The Damien Center serves more than 13,700 people annually. Along with mpox vaccinations, the facility provides HIV care, primary medical and dental care, food assistance, and housing support. The center primarily focuses on helping at-risk and low-income populations. Witchey also warned the community to be cautious of online misinformation. “There’s so much misinformation out there,” Witchey said. “For diseases, you want to go to things like the CDC website or to a trusted partner like the Damien Center.” Some services at the center require appointments, while others are available for walk-ins. 世界卫生组织称,目前欧洲八个国家报告有猴痘 - Yeni Safak

Monkeypox now reported in eight countries in Europe, says WHO - Yeni Safak English
新闻简报世界卫生组织 (WHO) 报告称,欧洲八个国家已发现猴痘,包括比利时、法国、德国、意大利、葡萄牙、西班牙、瑞典和英国,以及澳大利亚、加拿大和美国。这些病例被描述为非典型病例,大多数人没有前往西非或中非流行地区的旅行史。世卫组织区域主任汉斯·克鲁格强调,病毒通过密切的身体接触(包括性接触)传播,而且大多数病例都是轻微的。他还警告说,夏季可能会因大规模集会而导致 传播 增加。世卫组织正在与受影响国家合作,追踪 暴发 的来源、了解传播情况,并采取措施防止进一步出现 暴发。正在提供有关 监测、检测和感染预防的指南,以支持受影响的社区和卫生系统。

地点与对象
比利时、法国、德国、意大利、葡萄牙、西班牙、瑞典和英国;澳大利亚、加拿大和美国。
事件
据世界卫生组织 (WHO) 称,八个欧洲国家以及澳大利亚、加拿大和美国报告了猴痘病例。
规模、影响与风险
世界卫生组织欧洲区域至少有八个国家报告了猴痘病例,其中大多数病例较轻。世界卫生组织指出,这种疾病在某些弱势群体中可能更为严重。
应对、状态与不确定性
世卫组织正在与有关国家合作,进一步调查病例,确定可能的感染源、病毒的传播方式,并进一步限制 传播。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Monkeypox now reported in eight countries in Europe, says WHO - Yeni Safak English

News Brief
The World Health Organization (WHO) reported that monkeypox has been identified in eight countries across Europe, including Belgium, France, Germany, Italy, Portugal, Spain, Sweden, and the UK, as well as in Australia, Canada, and the US. The cases are described as atypical, with most individuals having no travel history to endemic regions in West or Central Africa. WHO regional director Hans Kluge emphasized that the virus spreads through close physical contact, including sexual contact, and that most cases are mild. He also warned that the summer season could lead to increased transmission due to mass gatherings. The WHO is collaborating with affected nations to trace the source of the outbreak, understand the spread, and implement measures to prevent further transmission. Guidance on surveillance, testing, and infection prevention is being provided to support affected communities and health systems.

Advertisement Home World Europe Monkeypox now reported in eight countries in Europe, says WHO Monkeypox now reported in eight countries in Europe, says WHO 10:30, 21/05/2022, SaturdayU: Update: 10:30, 21/05/2022, Saturday AA See Yeni Şafak News More Often: Click and Add to Favorites on Google! File photo Rare viral infection that can spread from person to person, caused by monkeypox virus, also reported in Australia, Canada, US At least eight countries in the World Health Organization European Region have reported cases of monkeypox in recent days, while Australia, Canada, and the US have also reported similar cases, the WHO said Friday. The European countries that in recent days reported monkeypox – a rare viral infection that can be spread from person to person and is caused by the monkeypox virus – are Belgium, France, Germany, Italy, Portugal, Spain, Sweden, and the UK. "These recent cases are atypical for several reasons," said Hans Kluge, WHO regional director for Europe. "In this instance, all but one of the recent cases have no relevant travel history to areas where monkeypox is endemic, in West Africa or Central Africa," he said. Kluge also said that most of the initial cases found were detected through sexual health services and are among men who had sex with men. "Because of the geographically dispersed nature of the cases across Europe and beyond, the transmission may have been ongoing for some time," he said. Kluge said WHO is working with concerned countries to investigate cases further, determine the likely source of infection, how the virus is spreading, and limit further transmission. "We are also providing guidance and support on surveillance, testing, infection prevention and control, clinical management, risk communication, and community engagement, as well as facilitating information sharing across countries and health networks," said Kluge. He said most of the cases currently under investigation in Europe are mild. - Self-limiting illness Monkeypox is usually a self-limiting illness, and most of those infected will recover within a few weeks without treatment. "However, the disease can be more severe, especially in young children, pregnant women, and immunocompromised individuals," said the WHO regional chief. According to Kluge, people with suspected monkeypox should be investigated and isolated on the first appearance of symptoms. "Monkeypox does not normally spread easily between humans, and requires close contact, with the virus entering the body through broken skin, the respiratory tract, eyes, nose and mouth, and via bodily fluids," he said. It can spread through close physical contact, including sexual contact, with someone who has monkeypox. "As we enter the summer season in the European Region, with mass gatherings, festivals, and parties, I am concerned that transmission could accelerate, as the cases currently being detected are among those engaging in sexual activity, and the symptoms are unfamiliar to many," said Kluge. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

猴痘 意识帮助蒙泽母亲保护她的孩子 - UNICEF

Mpox Awareness Helps a Monze Mother Protect Her Children - UNICEF
新闻简报2026年8月,由联合国儿童基金会支持、美国疾病预防控制中心资助的社区志愿者(CBV)项目帮助赞比亚蒙泽区的一位母亲识别了孩子的猴痘症状并及时寻求医疗护理。此次干预行动于 2026 年 4 月至 6 月期间进行,覆盖了南部省四个地区的 1,588 户家庭和 8,587 人。Chisekese 农村卫生中心的卫生工作者证实,孩子们的病情并非 猴痘,但他们因皮疹而接受了治疗。该计划强调早期发现和社区参与,对 CBV 进行培训以识别 猴痘 的体征和症状并鼓励及时就医。省卫生厅长指出,在此期间捕获了 19 例疑似病例,但没有一例符合 猴痘 病例定义。这一举措加强了 监测 疾病的防治,并展示了合作伙伴关系在向社区提供健康信息方面的价值。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026年8月(活动日期),发布于2026-08-14(发布日期)。
地点与对象
赞比亚南部省蒙泽区;Chisekese 流域内约 1,588 户家庭和 8,587 人,其中包括卡车司机、村长、孕妇和五岁以下儿童的母亲。
事件
联合国儿童基金会在美国疾病预防控制中心的资助下开展了一项社区志愿者 (CBV) 项目,帮助蒙泽区的一位母亲卡伦·穆廷塔 (Caren Mutinta) 识别孩子的 猴痘 症状并及时寻求医疗护理。Chisekese农村卫生中心的卫生工作者确认孩子们的病情并非猴痘,但他们接受了皮疹治疗并服用药物。
规模、影响与风险
干预覆盖南方省四个区,覆盖1,588户、8,587人。2026年4月至2026年6月期间,抓获19例疑似猴痘病例,但均不符合猴痘的病例定义。该计划强调早期发现和及时就医,从而降低了社区中未确诊 猴痘 病例的风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
该计划于 2026 年 4 月至 6 月期间实施,社区志愿者挨家挨户开展宣传活动。省卫生厅长确认干预措施在加强社区意识和早期发现方面取得了成功。该计划涉及卫生工作者、CBV、社区领袖和合作伙伴之间的合作。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Mpox Awareness Helps a Monze Mother Protect Her Children - UNICEF

News Brief
In August 2026, a community-based volunteer (CBV) program supported by UNICEF and funded by the US CDC helped a mother in Monze District, Zambia, recognize Mpox symptoms in her children and seek timely medical care. The intervention, which took place between April and June 2026, reached 1,588 households and 8,587 people across four districts in Southern Province. Health workers at Chisekese Rural Health Centre confirmed the children's condition was not Mpox, but they were treated for rashes. The program emphasized early detection and community engagement, with CBVs trained to identify signs and symptoms of Mpox and encourage prompt care-seeking. The Provincial Health Director noted that 19 suspected cases were captured during the period, but none met the Mpox case definition. This initiative strengthened disease surveillance and demonstrated the value of partnerships in delivering health information to communities.
Original source text (English)

Show results from: Clear search input Search for result Max Clear search input Search for result Article Mpox Awareness Helps a Monze Mother Protect Her Children How Mpox awareness is helping families recognise symptoms early, seek care promptly and protect their children and communities Tikulirekuti Banda UNICEF Zambia/2026/Mwenya 14 August 2026 MONZE, Southern Province, Zambia, August 2026 --- In Kazombo community, Monze District, Caren Mutinta, a mother of two, says she had never heard about Mpox until community-based volunteers (CBVs) visited her household. The visit was part of an integrated risk communication, community engagement and community-based surveillance intervention supported by UNICEF, with funding from the US Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (US CDC), to strengthen Mpox preparedness and early detection in Southern Province. Implemented between April and June 2026, the intervention covered Monze, Kazungula, Livingstone and Chikankata districts. “Before the community people came to sensitize us, we hadn’t known about Mpox,” Caren recalls. “After sensitization, we came to know what Mpox looks like.” During the household visit, the volunteers noticed rashes on her two children. Because they had been trained to identify signs and symptoms that could resemble Mpox, they advised her to take the children to the clinic immediately. When these people came to sensitize us on Mpox, they found my children with symptoms that looked like Mpox, Caren further says. I was told to take my children to the clinic. Then I took them the same day. At Chisekese Rural Health Centre, health workers assessed the children and confirmed that the condition was not Mpox. The children were treated for rashes and given medicine. “They were treated and given medicine,” Caren says. “From that time, there has been no other problem; they are okay.” UNICEF Zambia/2026/Mwenya Caren Mutinta and her family during an interview with UNICEF staff Tikulirekuti Banda. For Chinyama Miriam Buku, Facility In-Charge at Chisekese Rural Health Centre, this example shows how community sensitization strengthened early detection and timely care-seeking. “Through the programme, we were able to identify suspected cases early and support timely referral and treatment,” she says. “We were able to do this because of the programme.” UNICEF Zambia/2026/Mwenya Chinyama Mirriam Buku, Health Facility In Charge, Chisekese rural health centre, Monze. She adds that CBVs played a critical role in reaching households directly with lifesaving information. In the Chisekese catchment area, the programme reached approximately 1,588 households and 8,587 people, while also sensitizing truck drivers and engaging headmen, pregnant women, and mothers of children under five. “The programme was a success,” Buku says. “Our community is now able to identify the signs and symptoms of Mpox, and we are confident they will report to the facility if they suspect anything related to Mpox.” The CBVs played a critical role in taking lifesaving Mpox information directly to households and strengthening community-based surveillance. Through the training, CBVs were equipped to identify possible signs and symptoms of Mpox and explain how it spreads and can be prevented and encourage families to seek care early when they notice symptoms of concern. For Cletus Mukonka, one of the trained CBVs, the training gave volunteers the confidence to move from house to house and engage communities with clear and accurate information. We were trained in Mpox by UNICEF, and after being trained, we were deployed in the community to start sensitization on Mpox: the signs and symptoms, how it spreads, and how we can prevent it. UNICEF Zambia/2026/Mwenya Cletus Mukonka and Judith Biemba, Community based volunteers conducting door to door sensitizations in Kazombo village Monze. For Sebastian Siyamwenga, village headman of Kazombo, engaging local leaders early helped strengthen community acceptance of the Mpox sensitization activities. UNICEF Zambia/2026/Mwenya Sebastian Siyamwenga, village headman of Kazombo village in Monze. We thank the health facility for engaging us in this programme to sensitize the community on the signs and symptoms of Mpox. We are very happy that they engaged us early enough in this programme. At provincial level, the intervention also demonstrated the value of community-based surveillance, timely risk communication, and collaboration between health workers, CBVs, community leaders, and partners. “Between April and June, Southern Province captured 19 suspected cases of Mpox, and all these cases were referred to health facilities. Although surveillance was heightened, none of the 19 cases met the case definition of Mpox,” Dr. Calistus Kayunga, Provincial Health Director, Southern Province said. As final remarks, the Provincial Health Director said the intervention showed the value of strong partnerships in bringing lifesaving information and services closer to communities. UNICEF Zambia/2026/Mwenya Tikulirekuti Banda-UNICEF, with Dr. Calistus Kayunga, Provincial Health Director, Southern Province. “The reach and impact of this project cannot be overemphasized. It made a huge difference within a short period by reaching our people and showing what can be achieved when we work together with partners. Through strong collaboration, we can design and implement interventions that positively impact the communities we serve,” Dr. Calistus Kayunga said. For Caren Mutinta, the impact was immediate and personal: she received information, acted quickly, and her children were treated on time. Her experience shows how integrated door-to-door sensitizations can move communities from limited awareness to early action, protecting families while strengthening disease surveillance at community level. 事件

Skip to main content Zambia Toggle navigation Visit UNICEF Global Zambia Explore UNICEF Supply UNICEF About us Children in Zambia Job vacancies Meet the Rep Contact us Press centre Donate Zambia What we do Research and reports Stories Take action Search area has closed. Show results from: Clear search input Search for result Max Clear search input Search for result Article Reaching People on the Move with Mpox Information That Protects Through tailored risk communication, communities on the move are gaining the knowledge they need to prevent mpox and seek care when symptoms arise Tikulirekuti Banda UNICEF Zambia/2026/Mwenya 14 August 2026 MONZE District, Zambia, August 2026 --- Across Southern Province, long-distance truck drivers move goods between Zambia and neighbouring countries including South Africa, Botswana, Namibia, Zimbabwe and the Democratic Republic of Congo (DRC). As they travel long distances and interact with different people along transport routes, they can face increased exposure to public health risks. Thus, drivers need clear and practical information on public health risks including diseases like Mpox. They require information on how to protect themselves from and prevent themselves from catching diseases and on recognizing symptoms early and seeking care in time. UNICEF, with support from the US Centers for Disease Control and Prevention, partnered with the Ministry of Health through the Southern Provincial Health Office to implement integrated Mpox activities between April and June 2026. The activities focused on communities along the international trucking route from Southern Africa towards the mining towns in the Copperbelt and Northwestern Zambia. The response package included risk communication, community engagement, community-based surveillance, and hygiene promotion and infection prevention and control in Chikankata, Monze, Livingstone and Kazungula, districts that sit along an international trucking route from Southern Africa. Chikankata district lies on the trucking route and hosts the Zambia Revenue Authority’s truckers’ dry port, these factors made it one of the obvious support site. UNICEF Zambia/2026/Mwenya Dr. Kanunga Kazhila, Acting District Health Director, Chikankata District Health Office. We conducted this programme as Chikankata district. We are a transit district with a transit population whose composition is full of traders and people who are both from within the country and out of the country. The programme was beneficial in that every community was sensitised about the importance of looking out for the signs and symptoms of Mpox. For drivers such as Joseph Muzikiti, the sensitization offered information that could be used immediately and passed on to others. Joseph, who has driven through South Africa, Botswana, Zimbabwe, Zambia and the DRC for nine years, said the knowledge was useful because drivers spend much of their time moving across borders. “I have learnt a lot about Mpox,” Joseph said. “You know, us truck drivers, we travel a lot, and we are at risk of too much Mpox. The knowledge that I have about Mpox now, I can even teach the other drivers about it.” UNICEF Zambia/2026/Mwenya Long-distance truck driver Joseph Muzikiti poses in front of his truck in Monze. The message also helped drivers move from general awareness to practical prevention. White Paiva first heard about Mpox while travelling in the DRC, where he understood it mainly as a contagious disease. UNICEF Zambia/2026/Mwenya White Paiva, truck driver speaking with the community-based volunteers from his truck in Southern Province. I first heard about Mpox in Congo DRC and knew it was contagious. But I did not know that simple hygiene and prevention practices could reduce the risk of infection. Now I know that, as truck drivers, we need to recognize symptoms early, avoid close contact with suspected cases, maintain hygiene and seek care quickly if we suspect illness. For Lovely Mukupa, who has worked as a truck driver for 19 years, the session filled an important information gap. “This is the best programme because you are educating us on how not to contract the disease and how it can be treated,” Lovely said. “It is the first time someone is educating us on Mpox.” Her experience reflects the value of bringing trusted health information directly to drivers who may not always be reached through routine communication channels. UNICEF Zambia/2026/Mwenya White Paiva, truck driver with Community Based Volunteers from Nansenga health facility, Chikankanta district Together, these voices show why Mpox risk communication is critical for mobile populations. For truck drivers, long journeys, cross-border movement, and regular interaction with different people can increase exposure to communicable disease risks. By prioritizing drivers with clear information on symptoms, prevention, hygiene and when to seek care, UNICEF and partners helped ensure that lifesaving messages travel with the people who keep goods moving across the region. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

广州八院唐小平、于海生团队关于猴痘病毒的最新研究 - 搜狐网

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报广州市第八人民医院和山西农业大学研究团队发表了关于MPXV Clade Ib在实验室小鼠模型中致病特征的研究。该研究比较了感染Clade Ib和Clade IIb的不同品系小鼠的疾病结局和免疫应答,并对抗病毒药物药物特考韦瑞进行了评价。研究结果表明,Clade Ib 在小鼠中引起了更严重的疾病结果,Stat1 基因敲除小鼠在第 8 天时表现出 100% 特考韦瑞。该研究还强调了 特考韦瑞 在这些模型中减少病毒复制和疾病严重程度的潜力。该研究表明,Clade Ib 对免疫功能低下的个体构成更大的威胁,并可能产生更严重的临床结果。该研究是在实验室环境中使用小鼠模型进行的,结果基于实验数据,而不是现实世界的 暴发 或公共卫生反应。
查看新闻五要素
时间
The study was published on 2026-08-16. The research findings were based on data from 2022 and 2023, with the experimental results from the study conducted in 2026.
地点与对象
该研究使用小鼠模型进行,包括C57BL/6、BALB/c、Stat1基因敲除小鼠和AGB6小鼠,这些都是实验动物。该研究重点关注猴痘病毒 (MPXV) Ib 分支及其对这些模型的影响。
事件
广州市第八人民医院和山西农业大学研究团队发表了关于MPXV Clade Ib在实验室小鼠模型中致病特征的研究。该研究比较了感染Clade Ib和Clade IIb的不同品系小鼠的疾病结局和免疫应答,并对抗病毒药物药物特考韦瑞进行了评价。
规模、影响与风险
研究发现,与 Clade IIb 相比,Clade Ib 对小鼠造成了更严重的疾病结果,Stat1 基因敲除小鼠在第 8 天时表现出 100% 特考韦瑞。该研究还强调了 特考韦瑞 在这些模型中减少病毒复制和疾病严重程度的潜力。研究结果表明,Clade Ib 对免疫功能低下的个体构成更大的威胁,并可能产生更严重的临床结果。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究是在实验室环境中使用小鼠模型进行的。研究小组建立了小鼠模型系统来评估MPXV Clade Ib的致病性和抗病毒药物效应。结果基于实验数据,与现实世界的 暴发 或公共卫生反应无关。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Chinese)
广州八院唐小平、于海生团队关于猴痘病毒的最新研究 - 搜狐网
News Brief
A research team from Guangzhou Eighth People's Hospital and Shanxi Agricultural University published a study on the pathogenic characteristics of MPXV Clade Ib in laboratory mouse models. The study compared the disease outcomes and immune responses of different mouse strains infected with Clade Ib and Clade IIb, and evaluated the antiviral drug tecovirimat. The findings showed that Clade Ib caused more severe disease outcomes in mice, with Stat1 gene knockout mice showing 100% mortality by day 8. The research also highlighted the potential of tecovirimat to reduce viral replication and disease severity in these models. The study suggests that Clade Ib poses a greater threat to immunocompromised individuals and may have more severe clinical outcomes. The research was conducted in a laboratory setting using mouse models, and the results are based on experimental data, not real-world outbreaks or public health responses.
Original source text (Chinese)
顶部 首页 首页 晴 / 21° 隐私 广州八院唐小平、于海生团队关于猴痘病毒的最新研究 病毒学界 近日,广州医科大学附属市八医院唐小平教授、于海生教授团队联合山西农业大学动物医学学院庞全海团队,在《BMC Microbiology》发表题为“Pathogenic Signatures and Therapeutic Evaluation of Emergent MPXV Clade Ib in Low-Susceptibility and Immunocompromised Mouse Models” 研究论文。 该研究围绕新发的猴痘病毒Clade Ib,建立了覆盖不同遗传背景和免疫状态的小鼠感染模型(C57BL/6小鼠、 BALB/c小鼠、Stat1基因敲除小鼠和AGB6小鼠),系统比较了不同小鼠对猴痘病毒的易感性、宿主免疫转录特征以及Clade Ib与Clade IIb的致病差异,并进一步评价了抗病毒药物特考玮瑞的抗病毒效果,为猴痘病毒Clade Ib的致病机制研究和抗病毒干预评价提供了可重复、易操作的实验平台。 (新发Clade Ib带来新的公共卫生挑战) 展开剩余 85 % 四种小鼠呈现不同感染结局 研究团队采用鼻猴痘病毒主要分为Clade I和Clade II,其中又可进一步划分为Ia、Ib、IIa和IIb等亚分支。 2022年全球猴痘疫情主要是Clade IIb,而2023年在刚果出现了Clade Ib,随后持续传播并扩散至非洲以外地区。与Clade IIb相比,Clade Ib被认为与更严重的临床结局和持续的人际传播有关,对儿童、孕妇及免疫功能低下人群构成更大威胁。 然而,传统非人灵长类动物模型成本高、实验周期长,CAST/EiJ等易感小鼠又存在繁殖困难、供应受限等问题。因此,建立来源稳定、操作方便且能够反映不同疾病严重程度的小鼠模型,特别是建立评价疫苗小鼠模型,是开展猴痘病毒研究的迫切需求。 内接种方式,以相同剂量的MPXV Clade Ib感染四种不同遗传背景的小鼠(C57BL/6小鼠、 BALB/c小鼠、Stat1基因敲除小鼠和AGB6小鼠),结果显示,四种小鼠感染后均出现不同程度的体重下降,但疾病结局存在明显差异。 其中,STAT1缺失小鼠表现出最高易感性,在感染后第8天前后达到100%死亡;AGB6小鼠死亡率约为40%。相比之下,C57BL/6和BALB/c小鼠虽然出现一过性体重下降,但均能够存活并逐渐恢复,表现为自限性感染。 病毒学和病理学检测进一步发现,病毒能够在小鼠鼻甲和肺组织中有效复制。肺组织中的病毒抗原主要分布于支气管上皮及其周围炎症细胞,STAT1缺失小鼠的病毒抗原信号和肺部损伤最为明显。这些结果提示,宿主干扰素信号通路是限制Clade Ib复制和疾病进展的重要防线。 (Stat1敲除小鼠、AGB6 小鼠与野生型小鼠对猴痘病毒 Ib 分支的易感性差异) 不同遗传背景决定不同免疫响应节奏 为了进一步解析宿主免疫反应,研究团队对感染后不同时间点的C57BL/6和BALB/c小鼠脾脏进行了转录组测序。 结果显示,C57BL/6小鼠能够较早启动强烈的抗病毒反应。感染后第3天,便可检测到大量差异表达基因,Oas1a、Rtp1和Ligp1等抗病毒相关基因明显上调;感染后第5天,差异表达基因数量达到峰值。相比之下,BALB/c小鼠的转录反应出现得更晚,整体幅度也相对较弱。 值得注意的是,尽管两种小鼠均激活了干扰素和炎症相关通路,但其适应性免疫相关基因却出现不同程度下调。 C57BL/6小鼠主要表现为淋巴细胞分化和功能相关通路受抑,而BALB/c小鼠则更多表现为MHC抗原加工与呈递通路下调。 进入恢复期后,C57BL/6小鼠逐渐启动细胞增殖、细胞外基质重塑和组织修复相关程序;BALB/c小鼠则在较晚阶段出现免疫球蛋白介导的免疫反应。 这说明,Clade Ib感染不仅能诱导强烈的先天免疫和炎症反应,还可能伴随适应性免疫相关程序的暂时抑制,而这种免疫失衡具有明显的宿主遗传背景差异。 (C57BL/6与Balb/c小鼠感染猴痘病毒Ib分支后脾脏转录组的时间动态变化) Clade Ib表现出更快的感染进程 研究团队进一步在易感的Stat1缺失小鼠中,以相同感染剂量和相同实验条件比较了Clade Ib与Clade IIb的毒力。 结果显示,与Clade IIb相比,Clade Ib感染后小鼠体重下降出现得更早,疾病进展更快,肺组织病毒载量更高,病毒抗原分布也更加广泛。 转录组分析发现,Clade Ib感染伴随更明显的组织损伤和炎症相关表达特征,包括Mmp8和S100a3等基因上调。同时,干扰素应答、抗原加工与呈递等免疫相关通路呈现更广泛的下调。 不过,在所检测时间点的肺组织病理评分并未出现显著差异。综上所述,现有结果支持Clade Ib在该模型中具有更快的感染动力学和更强的复制能力。 (Stat1 基因敲除小鼠中猴痘病毒Ib 分支与 IIb 分支的致病性比较) 特考玮瑞显著抑制病毒复制 为了验证这些小鼠模型是否可用于抗病毒药物评价,研究团队选择正痘病毒抑制剂特考玮瑞进行体内实验。 在C57BL/6、AGB6和Stat1缺失小鼠中,特考玮瑞均能够减轻感染后的体重下降,并明显降低肺组织中的病毒载量和病毒抗原水平。 组织病理学结果也显示,药物处理后小鼠肺组织结构保存更完整,炎症、坏死及其他感染相关损伤得到改善。 转录组结果表明,特考玮瑞治疗还可减弱病毒感染引起的大规模转录重塑,使宿主转录状态向创伤修复和恢复相关程序转变。 需要注意的是,本研究中的特考玮瑞在感染当天即开始给药,属于非常早期的干预模式。 (验证AGB6、Stat1缺失以及C57BL/6 小鼠作为猴痘病毒 Ib 分支药物评价模型的适用性) 研究意义 本研究建立了野生型、干扰素受体缺失和STAT1缺失小鼠Clade Ib感染的模型体系。 不同模型可满足不同研究需求: 野生型小鼠适合用于研究非致死性病毒复制、宿主免疫反应,以及药物和疫苗评价; AGB6小鼠可用于研究中度至重度感染; Stat1缺失小鼠则能够形成快速进展的致死性疾病,适合开展致病性和抗病毒药物的初步体内评价。 研究还从转录组层面揭示了Clade Ib感染过程中“先天免疫和炎症激活-适应性免疫相关程序下调-组织修复启动”的动态变化,为理解宿主遗传背景、干扰素信号和疾病严重程度之间的关系提供了实验数据。 作者信息 广州医科大学附属市八医院与山西农业大学联合培养博士生韩红宇、硕士生王肖玮为该论文共同第一作者,广州医科大学附属市八医院唐小平教授、于海生教授和山西农业大学庞全海教授为本文的共同通讯作者。 于海生教授,广州医科大学附属市八医院PI,传染病研究所副所长(主持工作),生物安全三级实验室副主任。长期从事新突发病毒的感染与免疫相关研究,致力于树突状细胞在病毒感染中作用、中和抗体研发以及研究高致病性病毒的致病机理。以第一作者或通讯作者在Molecular Therapy(2026)、National Science Review(2025)、Signal Transduction and Targeted Therapy(2023)、Nature Communications(2023)、JCI Insight(2020)等杂志发表 SCI 文章50余篇。主持或参与国家传染病重大专项、省自然科学基金、科技攻关专项等项目。 Free web novels for online reading. 评论 全部 还没有人评论过,快来抢首评 抢首评 推荐阅读 39健康网 前天12:10 9.3万 PNAS | 王奇慧、高福研究团队揭示SARS-CoV-2 NTD抗体免疫逃逸新机制 病毒学界 5天前 107 网站地图 X
View five news elements
Time
The study was published on 2026-08-16. The research findings were based on data from 2022 and 2023, with the experimental results from the study conducted in 2026.
Location and population
The research was conducted using mouse models, including C57BL/6, BALB/c, Stat1 gene knockout, and AGB6 mice, which are laboratory animals. The study focused on the monkeypox virus (MPXV) Clade Ib and its impact on these models.
Event
A research team from Guangzhou Eighth People's Hospital and Shanxi Agricultural University published a study on the pathogenic characteristics of MPXV Clade Ib in laboratory mouse models. The study compared the disease outcomes and immune responses of different mouse strains infected with Clade Ib and Clade IIb, and evaluated the antiviral drug tecovirimat.
Scale, impact and risk
The study found that Clade Ib caused more severe disease outcomes in mice compared to Clade IIb, with Stat1 gene knockout mice showing 100% mortality by day 8. The research also highlighted the potential of tecovirimat to reduce viral replication and disease severity in these models. The findings suggest that Clade Ib poses a greater threat to immunocompromised individuals and may have more severe clinical outcomes.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study was conducted in a laboratory setting using mouse models. The research team established a system of mouse models to evaluate the pathogenicity and antiviral effects of MPXV Clade Ib. The results are based on experimental data and are not related to real-world outbreaks or public health responses.
原文 / Source
Published: 2026-08-11  |  Publisher: Associazione Corvelva

News Brief
The news report from Associazione Corvelva, published on August 11, 2026, focuses on the safety of vaccines, specifically the MMR and MMRV vaccines, and questions whether they can cause injury or death. It does not report any confirmed or suspected cases of mpox or related diseases. The content is general and informational, raising awareness about vaccine safety without providing specific data on mpox outbreaks, cases, or risk assessments. The report does not mention any investigation, testing, or response measures related to mpox or its vaccines. It is a general inquiry into vaccine safety, not a specific outbreak or health event. The content is in Italian and does not provide any explicit risk statements or case counts for mpox.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

下一次大流行会出现在医院还是伦敦街道下? - AOL.co.uk

Will the next pandemic be spotted in a hospital or under London’s streets? - AOL.co.uk
新闻简报The United Kingdom is scaling up environmental surveillance to detect priority pathogens, including mpox, as part of pandemic preparedness. UK Health Security Agency (UKHSA) is investing £1.3 million to test wastewater for genetic traces of mpox and West Nile virus, while ministers plan to expand monitoring across sewers, hospitals, and transport hubs, including aircraft toilets. This initiative follows earlier wastewater detections of vaccine-derived poliovirus in London, which did not require public health intervention. Experts note wastewater can provide early signals of community-level infection without individual testing, but emphasize it must be combined with clinical and laboratory confirmation. The approach aims to identify emerging threats before they escalate, though challenges remain in distinguishing meaningful signals from background noise.
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表于2026-08-13;该事件被描述为废水 监测 系统的持续规划和扩展,历史参考了 2022 年伦敦脊髓灰质炎废水检测。
地点与对象
英国,特别关注伦敦和希思罗机场、布里斯托尔机场和爱丁堡机场;人口层面的 监测 针对社区和旅行者。
事件
英国正在扩大环境 监测 系统(废水、医院、运输),以检测包括 猴痘 和西尼罗河病毒在内的优先病原体,作为大流行防范的一部分;UKHSA 正在测试废水中 猴痘 和其他病原体的遗传痕迹。这是一项指导和准备举措,而不是已确认的 暴发。
规模、影响与风险
所提供的证据中未报告有关 猴痘 特定病例、死亡或暴露的信息。废水 监测 覆盖 31 个地点,含有 12 种重点病原体;飞机废水 监测 在疫情期间对三个机场的 32 架飞机进行了评估。未说明 猴痘 特定规模或风险级别。
应对、状态与不确定性
UKHSA is spending £1.3 million to test wastewater for mpox and West Nile virus; ministers plan to expand environmental surveillance across sewers, hospitals, and transport settings. Wastewater analysis is described as one tool in a broader surveillance arsenal; limitations include difficulty in signal detection without known targets and the need for clinical confirmation. B routine priorityPublic-health news

Will the next pandemic be spotted in a hospital or under London’s streets? - AOL.co.uk

News Brief
The United Kingdom is scaling up environmental surveillance to detect priority pathogens, including mpox, as part of pandemic preparedness. UK Health Security Agency (UKHSA) is investing £1.3 million to test wastewater for genetic traces of mpox and West Nile virus, while ministers plan to expand monitoring across sewers, hospitals, and transport hubs, including aircraft toilets. This initiative follows earlier wastewater detections of vaccine-derived poliovirus in London, which did not require public health intervention. Experts note wastewater can provide early signals of community-level infection without individual testing, but emphasize it must be combined with clinical and laboratory confirmation. The approach aims to identify emerging threats before they escalate, though challenges remain in distinguishing meaningful signals from background noise.
Original source text (English)
Will the next pandemic be spotted in a hospital or under London’s streets? You probably don’t need reminding that Covid brought the country and the capital to its knees. It snuck up on us thanks to the speed of modern-day air travel — putting London right in the crosshairs of the pandemic. Since then, time, effort, and political and scientific capital has been invested to try and give us a headstart on identifying, then heading off, the next pandemic before it begins. And much of this work focuses on what we flush down our toilets and what passes under Londoners’ feet every day through its sewers. The UK has built an end-to-end wastewater system across 31 sites, testing for 12 priority pathogens that could cause problems to public health. Over the next year, ministers want to expand environmental surveillance across sewers, hospitals and transport settings. A separate pandemic preparedness plan says the UK Health Security Agency (UKHSA) will explore sampling aircraft wastewater to detect diseases arriving in Britain. “Wastewater tells no lies — everything we do in society is reflected in the wastewater” Davey Jones, professor of environmental science and public health at Bangor University The Government’s UK Biological Security Strategy, published in July, sets out plans to connect different forms of disease surveillance, while UKHSA is spending £1.3 million testing whether wastewater can reveal genetic traces of pathogens including Mpox and West Nile virus. “Wastewater tells no lies,” says Davey Jones, professor of environmental science and public health at Bangor University. “Everything we do in society is reflected in the wastewater.” We already check wastewater for the presence of polio. Earlier this year, the tracking of wastewater found two indications of vaccine-derived poliovirus in sewage samples taken from London. That was important because Australia — with whom London has regular flight connections — had a small spate of similar results. But indications in such small numbers in London are routine, and didn’t require any public health intervention, authorities said. Constructing a viral family tree It was a very different situation in 2022, when genetically linked detections occurring time and time again suggested there was person-to-person transmission in north and east London. That outbreak prompted a vaccination campaign within areas of the capital. Polio is a special case because Britain has monitored sewage for it routinely since 2014. But it also shows that it’s possible for scientists to separate nascent outbreaks from the biological background noise. “You have the pathogen in the sample, so you can sequence it,” says Nicholas Grassly, professor of infectious disease and vaccine epidemiology at Imperial College London. With more than one detection, researchers can construct a viral family tree, he explains, helping them understand “where it’s come from”. But it’s harder than it first seems. “Picking up a signal is extremely difficult if you don’t know what you’re looking for,” says Barbara Kasprzyk-Hordern, who leads the University of Bath’s Centre of Excellence in Water-Based Early-Warning Systems for Health Protection. That means that wastewater analysis is just one weapon in the arsenal that needs to be used to tackle potential problems before they arise. While wastewater can indicate where officials should look, doctors and laboratories need to establish whether people are becoming ill and what treatment is needed. Grassly describes wastewater as “a free biological sample from hundreds of thousands of individuals”, one that can indicate how widespread an infection is without requiring each person to come forward for testing. The same sample can also be searched for several pathogens at once. That means health authorities can track common illnesses such as Covid, flu, respiratory syncytial virus (RSV), measles and polio while looking for rarer virus signatures in the background. A signal from the airplane loo Aircraft toilets could add another layer. In a 2022 UK study, researchers sampled sewage from terminals and 32 aircraft at Heathrow, Bristol and Edinburgh airports. At the peak of the pandemic, 93 per cent of aircraft samples contained SARS-CoV-2 — the virus that caused Covid. The Government now wants to evaluate whether similar methods could flag imported diseases without swabbing passengers individually — helping scale up detection of potential problems before they arise. The ability of wastewater to survey whole populations without identifying individuals is a massive advantage and can help identify from where outbreaks occurred, and help snub them out before they become significant. “We never know who is ill,” says Kasprzyk-Hordern. “But we know how communities were exposed.” Once signs that something is wrong appears in wastewater, conventional testing can be used to find out whether people are infected and whether an intervention is required. Grassly points out that there is another limitation. Finding a virus at Heathrow may buy Britain several days or perhaps a week compared with hospital detection — but it also means the pathogen is already travelling internationally. His Wastewater Surveillance for Pandemic Prevention network is therefore looking further upstream, in communities in Africa and South-east Asia where animal viruses repeatedly spill into people. Those small spillovers are “the sparks of the fire”, Grassly says. Detecting them early could allow safer market practices or interventions in livestock before an outbreak becomes a global emergency. “The biggest benefit will be when we align all the pieces of information together,” says Kasprzyk-Hordern, including what is happening at the clinical, community and environmental levels. “We’re not there yet.” But with work, it’s possible to be. As Grassly puts it, “prevention is going to be much easier than being responsive”. Event
The UK is expanding environmental surveillance systems (wastewater, hospitals, transport) to detect priority pathogens including mpox and West Nile virus as part of pandemic preparedness; UKHSA is testing wastewater for genetic traces of mpox and other pathogens. This is a guidance and preparedness initiative, not a confirmed outbreak.
Scale, impact and risk
